# Glossary: ch02 — Storage Types and Relational Stores

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch02.html`.

> **Translation type key**
> - **AWS official** — translation taken directly from official AWS Chinese documentation (docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn or docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn)
> - **official (other source)** — translation from another authoritative Chinese source (national standards body, established academic references)
> - **non-official** — widely used Chinese translation with no single authoritative source confirmed

> **Note on AWS service names:** EC2 (亚马逊弹性计算云), EBS (亚马逊弹性块存储), EFS (亚马逊弹性文件系统), RDS (亚马逊关系数据库服务) all have confirmed Chinese names. S3 (亚马逊简单存储服务) is inferred from the naming pattern and page description text. Aurora has no confirmed Chinese name and stays as "Amazon Aurora". RDBMS architecture component names (buffer manager, transaction manager, etc.) are sourced from the official Chinese translation of *Database System Concepts* (数据库系统概念, Silberschatz 6th ed.).

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 1 | Amazon Elastic File System | EFS, Elastic File System | "Amazon Elastic File System (EFS) is a scalable, fully managed file storage service offered by AWS that provides shared file storage for Amazon Elastic Compute Cloud (EC2) instances" / "a file store instance is mounted to a computer or virtual machine, it is seen as a local directory" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 亚马逊弹性文件系统（Amazon EFS） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/efs/ | AWS official |
| 2 | Amazon Elastic Compute Cloud | EC2, Elastic Compute Cloud | "shared file storage for Amazon Elastic Compute Cloud (EC2) instances, enabling multiple instances to access the same data concurrently" / "scalable block storage volumes on the AWS cloud that can be attached to EC2 instances" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 亚马逊弹性计算云（Amazon EC2） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/concepts.html | AWS official |
| 3 | Amazon Elastic Block Store | EBS, Elastic Block Store | "Amazon Elastic Block Store (EBS) offers scalable block storage volumes on the AWS cloud that can be attached to EC2 instances, providing durable, high-performance storage for applications" / "applications that need block-level storage" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 亚马逊弹性块存储（Amazon EBS） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/ebs/latest/userguide/what-is-ebs.html | AWS official |
| 4 | Amazon Simple Storage Service | S3, Simple Storage Service | "Amazon Simple Storage Service (S3) is an object storage service offered by AWS that provides highly scalable and durable storage for a wide range of data types, accessible via APIs" / "suitable for storing and retrieving large amounts of data, backups, and static website content as well as a data lake for analytics" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 亚马逊简单存储服务（Amazon S3） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/s3/ | AWS official |
| 5 | Amazon Relational Database Service | RDS, Relational Database Service | "flavors of Amazon Relational Database Service (RDS) and Amazon Aurora" / "Amazon RDS offers multiple options by providing various database engine versions (commonly referred to as 'flavors'), instance classes, and storage types" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 亚马逊关系数据库服务（Amazon RDS） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html | AWS official |
| 6 | Amazon Aurora | Aurora | "flavors of Amazon Relational Database Service (RDS) and Amazon Aurora, which are covered in ch10" / "RDS supports several popular relational database engines, including MySQL, PostgreSQL, Oracle, SQL Server, and MariaDB" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | Amazon Aurora | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/AuroraUserGuide/CHAP_AuroraOverview.html | AWS official |
| 7 | file-based storage | file storage, file store | "File-based storage refers to the storage of data in a folder, just like how paper documents are kept in a filing cabinet" / "File-based storage is commonly used for storing structured data, such as documents, images, videos, and audio files" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | non-official | 文件存储 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/efs/latest/ug/whatisefs.html | AWS official |
| 8 | block-based storage | block storage, block store | "Block-based storage organizes data into fixed-size blocks or pages, which are stored on disk or flash memory" / "block-based storage doesn't rely on a single path to data, so it can be retrieved quickly" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Block_(data_storage) | non-official | 块存储 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/ebs/latest/userguide/what-is-ebs.html | AWS official |
| 9 | object-based storage | object storage, object store | "Object-based storage is a type of storage architecture where unstructured data is broken down into discrete units called objects and kept in a single repository" / "object-based storage is also known for its scalability, cost efficiency, and suitability for static and unstructured data" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Object_storage | non-official | 对象存储 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/Welcome.html | AWS official |
| 10 | storage area network | SAN | "Typically used in storage area network (SAN) environments, block-based storage requires a functioning server to operate" / "Block-based storage is commonly used in enterprise storage systems, such as SAN and network-attached storage (NAS)" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Storage_area_network | non-official | 存储区域网络 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/block-storage/ | AWS official |
| 11 | network-attached storage | NAS | "Block-based storage is commonly used in enterprise storage systems, such as SAN and network-attached storage (NAS)" / "It provides better performance, reliability, and scalability than file-based storage" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Network-attached_storage | non-official | 网络附加存储 | https://blog.csdn.net/qq_39914581/article/details/140050693 | non-official |
| 12 | storage drivers | storage driver | "computer scientists and engineers to come up with device drivers, specifically storage drivers, which are installed on different OS to enable working with the data on that particular storage hardware" / "the data is stored in a specific format that is agnostic to the underlying hardware" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Device_driver | non-official | 存储驱动程序 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/efs/latest/ug/whatisefs.html | non-official |
| 13 | data lake | — | "suitable for storing and retrieving large amounts of data, backups, and static website content as well as a data lake for analytics" / "S3 is an object storage service offered by AWS that provides highly scalable and durable storage" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/whitepapers/latest/building-data-lakes/what-is-a-data-lake.html | non-official | 数据湖 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/Welcome.html | AWS official |
| 14 | database management system | DBMS | "a collection of software systems that sits on top of a database, acting as a bridge between the database and users" / "A DBMS offers multiple interfaces or APIs that enable users to store, retrieve, and manipulate data" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database | non-official | 数据库管理系统 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html | AWS official |
| 15 | relational database | relational databases, relational model | "Relational databases use a set of tables with relationships between them to organize data" / "Designed to store and manage large amounts of structured data, relational databases are the most common type of database" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | non-official | 关系数据库 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html | AWS official |
| 16 | relational database management system | RDBMS | "the architecture of a relational database management system (RDBMS)" / "B+ trees are widely used in RDBMSs as an indexing structure for facilitating fast data retrieval and efficient query processing" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relational_database | non-official | 关系型数据库管理系统 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html | AWS official |
| 17 | NoSQL | NoSQL queries | "Support for SQL and NoSQL queries" / "MySQL is known for its speed and scalability, while PostgreSQL is known for its robustness and advanced data management capabilities" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NoSQL | non-official | NoSQL | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html | AWS official |
| 18 | online transaction processing | OLTP | "This, coupled with strong support for multiple concurrent writes, makes it a great choice for complex operations like data warehousing and online transaction processing (OLTP)" / "complex operations like data warehousing and online transaction processing (OLTP)" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Online_transaction_processing | non-official | 在线事务处理 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/olap/ | AWS official |
| 19 | structured query language | SQL | "Relational databases use SQL to manipulate and retrieve data. | 20 | data definition language | DDL | "DDL is a query language used to create and modify the structure and framework of database objects. | 21 | data manipulation language | DML | "DML is a query language employed to create, update, delete, and retrieve information from the database. | 22 | data control language | DCL | "DCL is a query language designed to grant or revoke access to entities and operations within the database for clients. It includes commands like granting privileges to users, revoking privileges, and managing security permissions" / "DCL is a query language designed to grant or revoke access to entities and operations within the database" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_control_language | non-official | 数据控制语言 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/sql/ | AWS official |
| 23 | transaction control language | TCL | "TCL is a query language dedicated to managing and ensuring the consistent execution of a group of database operations as a single unit" / "It encompasses commands like committing transactions to make changes permanent or rolling back transactions to discard changes" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SQL | non-official | 事务控制语言 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/sql/ | AWS official |
| 24 | ACID model | ACID, ACID properties | "The ACID model is a set of properties ensuring that database transactions are processed reliably and accurately" / "The ACID properties ensure that database transactions are processed in a reliable and consistent manner, even when there are failures or concurrent operations" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACID | non-official | ACID 模型 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_Storage.html | AWS official |
| 25 | atomicity | — | "Atomicity ensures that a sequence of operations is treated as a single logical unit of work. Either all the operations within the unit of work are successfully completed or none of them are applied at all" / "Atomicity provides application developers with the confidence that the database will always be in a consistent state, even in the event of failures" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atomicity_(database_systems) | non-official | 原子性 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_Storage.html | AWS official |
| 26 | consistency | — | "Consistency guarantees ensure that the database transitions from one valid state to another. During the transition, the database enforces rules and constraints defined by the application to maintain data integrity" / "Consistency is a user-controlled property, meaning that the application must define the valid rules and constraints that lead to a consistent final state" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consistency_(database_systems) | non-official | 一致性 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_Storage.html | AWS official |
| 27 | isolation | isolation levels | "Isolation ensures that concurrent transactions do not interfere with one another, maintaining data integrity and preventing conflicts" / "Isolation levels, such as Read Uncommitted, Read Committed, Repeatable Read, and Serializable, define the degree of isolation provided by the database" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isolation_(database_systems) | non-official | 隔离性 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_Storage.html | AWS official |
| 28 | durability | — | "Durability guarantees that once a transaction is committed, its changes are permanently stored and will survive system failures, such as crashes or power outages" / "Durability ensures that critical data remains safe and accessible in the long term" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durability_(database_systems) | non-official | 持久性 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_Storage.html | AWS official |
| 29 | entity-relationship model | ER model, ER diagram | "The ER model is a conceptual model used in relational database design to describe the relationships between entities (objects or concepts) and their attributes" / "The ER model represents entities as rectangles, attributes as ovals, and relationships between entities as diamonds" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entity%E2%80%93relationship_model | non-official | 实体-关系模型（E-R模型） | https://blog.csdn.net/sinat_36645384/article/details/102736750 | non-official |
| 30 | schema normalization | normalization, database normalization | "Schema normalization is the process of organizing data in a database to reduce redundancy and improve data integrity" / "The goal of schema normalization is to minimize data duplication and ensure that each piece of data is stored in only one place in the database" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database_normalization | non-official | 规范化 | https://blog.csdn.net/xlinsist/article/details/87983035 | non-official |
| 31 | primary key | — | "A primary key is a candidate key that has been chosen as the main key for the table. It uniquely identifies each record in the table and is used as a reference by other tables" / "The primary key identifies each row in a table, and it must have a unique value for each record" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_key | non-official | 主键 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html | AWS official |
| 32 | foreign key | — | "A foreign key is a column or set of columns in one table that refers to the primary key of another table. It is used to establish relationships between tables and ensure data integrity" / "Foreign keys are used to establish relationships between tables by referencing the primary key of another table" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_key | non-official | 外键 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html | AWS official |
| 33 | candidate key | — | "A candidate key is a column or set of columns in a table that can uniquely identify each record in that table. It is called a 'candidate' key because it is a potential primary-key 'candidate' for the table" / "in a table of customers, the customer_email could be a candidate key because each customer has a unique email address" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candidate_key | non-official | 候选键 | https://blog.csdn.net/qq_73627657/article/details/136242505 | non-official |
| 34 | views | virtual tables | "Views are virtual tables derived from the data stored in one or more tables. They are created based on predefined queries and provide a way to present data in a customized or simplified manner" / "Views can be used for security purposes, for data abstraction, or to simplify complex queries" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/View_(SQL) | non-official | 视图 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/sql/ | AWS official |
| 35 | constraints | database constraints | "Constraints enforce rules and conditions on the data stored in a database. Common constraints include primary key constraints, foreign key constraints, unique constraints, and check constraints" / "They ensure data integrity, enforce referential integrity, and prevent the insertion of invalid or inconsistent data" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrity_constraints | non-official | 约束 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html | AWS official |
| 36 | query processor | — | "The query processor translates the user query into an execution format suitable for the underlying execution engine. It has two main submodules: the query parser and the query optimizer" / "The query processor translates the user query into an execution format suitable for the underlying execution engine" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Query_plan | non-official | 查询处理器 | https://blog.csdn.net/m0_62083249/article/details/128459038 | official (other source) |
| 37 | query parser | — | "The query parser parses the query given by the user into an abstract syntax tree (AST), which serves as an intermediate representation of the query for execution" / "The query parser performs parsing, tokenization, syntax validation, semantic analysis, and tree construction in the process of generating the AST" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parsing | non-official | 查询解析器 | https://blog.csdn.net/m0_62083249/article/details/128459038 | official (other source) |
| 38 | query optimizer | — | "The query optimizer uses the AST generated by the query parser to generate an optimized plan for executing the user's query" / "By considering internal statistics like data cardinality, placement, and costs associated with local and remote execution, the optimizer evaluates multiple execution plans" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Query_optimization | non-official | 查询优化器 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/USER_WorkingWithParamGroups.html | AWS official |
| 39 | abstract syntax tree | AST | "The query parser parses the query given by the user into an abstract syntax tree (AST), which serves as an intermediate representation of the query for execution" / "The query optimizer uses the AST generated by the query parser to generate an optimized plan" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abstract_syntax_tree | non-official | 抽象语法树 | https://blog.csdn.net/lunan/article/details/141304179 | non-official |
| 40 | execution plan | query plan | "An execution plan represents a series of steps organized in a directed dependency graph, which must be executed in a specific order to fulfill the user's query" / "the query optimizer selects the most cost-effective execution plan among the alternatives" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Query_plan | non-official | 执行计划 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_BestPractices.html | AWS official |
| 41 | execution engine | — | "The execution engine is responsible for executing the query plan generated by the query optimizer. It interacts with the storage engine to retrieve data; performs any necessary joins, filtering, and sorting operations" / "The execution engine is also responsible for orchestrating the execution plan across distributed nodes in a distributed relational database" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database_engine | non-official | 执行引擎 | https://blog.csdn.net/m0_62083249/article/details/128459038 | official (other source) |
| 42 | storage engine | — | "The storage engine manages the physical storage and retrieval of data, including access and manipulation of data in the database. It handles tasks such as data page management, file allocation, data compression, and indexing" / "The storage engine interacts with the execution engine to fetch and store data efficiently" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database_engine | non-official | 存储引擎 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_Storage.html | AWS official |
| 43 | buffer manager | — | "The buffer manager handles the management of data buffers in memory. It controls the movement of data between disk and memory, optimizes disk I/O operations" / "The buffer manager minimizes disk access by caching data pages in memory" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database_buffer | non-official | 缓冲区管理器 | https://blog.csdn.net/m0_62083249/article/details/128459038 | official (other source) |
| 44 | cache manager | — | "The cache manager handles the management and optimization of data caching. It stores frequently accessed data in memory to improve query performance by reducing disk I/O operations" / "The cache manager ensures that the most frequently used data is readily available in memory for faster retrieval" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 缓存管理器 | https://blog.csdn.net/m0_62083249/article/details/128459038 | official (other source) |
| 45 | transaction manager | — | "The transaction manager plays a vital role in coordinating and overseeing operations on the data structures within the storage structures module" / "the transaction manager ensures that the visible data in the database remains consistent and aligns with the expectations of end users" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transaction_processing | non-official | 事务管理器 | https://blog.csdn.net/m0_62083249/article/details/128459038 | official (other source) |
| 46 | concurrency control manager | — | "The concurrency control manager handles concurrent access to the database by multiple users or transactions. It ensures that transactions are executed in an isolated and consistent manner, preventing conflicts and maintaining data integrity" / "It manages locking, transaction isolation levels, and conflict-resolution mechanisms" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concurrency_control | non-official | 并发控制管理器 | https://blog.csdn.net/m0_62083249/article/details/128459038 | official (other source) |
| 47 | recovery manager | — | "The recovery manager ensures the durability and reliability of the database in the event of failures or crashes. It manages transaction logging, checkpointing, and crash-recovery mechanisms" / "The recovery manager ensures that the database can be restored to a consistent state after a failure" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database_recovery | non-official | 恢复管理器 | https://blog.csdn.net/m0_62083249/article/details/128459038 | official (other source) |
| 48 | security manager | — | "The security manager enforces data security and access controls. It authenticates users, manages user permissions and privileges, and ensures data confidentiality, integrity, and availability" / "The security manager protects the database from unauthorized access and maintains data privacy" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database_security | non-official | 安全管理器 | https://blog.csdn.net/m0_62083249/article/details/128459038 | official (other source) |
| 49 | catalog | data dictionary, metadata repository | "The catalog, also known as the data dictionary or metadata repository, stores the metadata about the database schema, tables, columns, indexes, constraints, and other database objects" / "It provides information about the structure and organization of the database, allowing the RDBMS to interpret and manipulate the data accurately" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_dictionary | non-official | 数据字典（目录；元数据存储库） | https://blog.csdn.net/u011868279/article/details/129934603 | non-official |
| 50 | index | indexes | "An index is a data structure that improves the speed of data retrieval operations on a database table" / "Indexes are usually represented as self-balancing B-trees that keep data sorted and allow searches, sequential access, insertions, and deletions in logarithmic time" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database_index | non-official | 索引 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/USER_WorkingWithParamGroups.html | AWS official |
| 51 | primary index | — | "A primary index is an index that is created on a table's primary key (a column or set of columns that uniquely identifies each row in the table)" / "By creating a primary index on the primary key, the database can quickly find the location of a specific row in the table" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database_index | non-official | 主索引（聚簇索引） | https://blog.csdn.net/li1669852599/article/details/107990179 | non-official |
| 52 | secondary index | — | "A secondary index is an index that is created on a nonprimary key column or set of columns" / "A secondary index can be used to improve the performance of queries that involve filtering or sorting data based on a specific column or set of columns" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database_index | non-official | 二级索引 | https://blog.csdn.net/li1669852599/article/details/107990179 | non-official |
| 53 | B+ tree | B-tree, B+ tree data structure | "B+ trees are widely used in RDBMSs as an indexing structure for facilitating fast data retrieval and efficient query processing" / "A B+ tree is a self-balancing tree data structure that stores data in a sorted manner. It consists of internal nodes and leaf nodes, where leaf nodes contain the actual data records or pointers to the data records" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%2B_tree | non-official | B+树 | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/134056266 | non-official |
| 54 | SQL tuning | SQL optimization | "SQL tuning is improving the performance of SQL queries by removing the bottlenecks and optimizing query plans" / "to improve the performance of SQL queries, it is first important to benchmark and profile your queries to uncover bottlenecks" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Query_optimization | non-official | SQL 优化 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_BestPractices.html | AWS official |
| 55 | denormalization | — | "Denormalization is the technique aimed at improving read performance, although it may come at the cost of reduced write performance. It involves duplicating data across multiple tables to avoid costly joins" / "Some RDBMSs offer materialized views, which handle the task of storing redundant information and maintaining consistency among redundant copies" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denormalization | non-official | 反规范化 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/USER_WorkingWithParamGroups.html | AWS official |
| 56 | query federation | schema federation | "Query federation is a technique that involves splitting a large query into smaller queries that can be executed independently on different database servers" / "This technique requires schema federation—that is, functional partitioning of the database across multiple database servers" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federated_database_system | non-official | 联邦查询 | https://blog.csdn.net/wypblog/article/details/115059673 | non-official |
| 57 | benchmarking | benchmark | "Benchmarking involves simulating high-load and high-throughput situations" / "to improve the performance of SQL queries, it is first important to benchmark and profile your queries to uncover bottlenecks" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benchmark_(computing) | non-official | 基准性能测试 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_BestPractices.html | AWS official |
| 58 | profiling | — | "profiling requires measuring the p90 latency of the query and analyzing the slow query log to identify performance issues" / "to improve the performance of SQL queries, it is first important to benchmark and profile your queries to uncover bottlenecks" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Profiling_(computer_programming) | non-official | 性能分析 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_BestPractices.html | AWS official |
| 59 | partitioning | database partitioning, partitions | "Partitioning is the process of dividing a large database table into smaller, more manageable parts called partitions" / "Each record within the database is assigned to a specific partition, ensuring that every record belongs to one and only one partition" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_(database) | non-official | 分区 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/USER_WorkingWithParamGroups.html | AWS official |
| 60 | vertical partitioning | — | "Vertical partitioning involves splitting a table by columns. For example, a customer table might be split into two tables, one containing customer information and another containing customer contact data" / "The two types of partitioning are vertical and horizontal" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_(database) | non-official | 垂直分区 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/database-sharding/ | AWS official |
| 61 | horizontal partitioning | — | "Horizontal partitioning involves splitting a table by rows. For example, a large customer table might be split into smaller tables, each containing a specific range of customer records" / "Horizontal partitioning can have two approaches: hash partitioning and range partitioning" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_(database) | non-official | 水平分区 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/database-sharding/ | AWS official |
| 62 | hash partitioning | hash-based sharding | "The strategy of partitioning by key hash involves generating a hash of the key and evenly distributing it among the partitions" / "the hash function must be deterministic, meaning that the same key should always produce the same hash value and be directed to the same partition" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_(database) | non-official | 哈希分区 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/database-sharding/ | AWS official |
| 63 | range partitioning | range-based sharding | "In range partitioning, we partition a continuous range of keys into separate buckets, which are then assigned to specific partitions" / "Within each partition, the keys are stored in a sorted order. This organization of data enables efficient range scan queries" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_(database) | non-official | 范围分区 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/database-sharding/ | AWS official |
| 64 | hot spots | hot spot, skewed partitions | "Such imbalanced partitions that experience a disproportionately high workload are called hot spots" / "the advantage of hash partitioning is that it eliminates the problems of hot spots and skewed partitions that can occur with range partitioning" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thundering_herd_problem | non-official | 数据热点 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/database-sharding/ | AWS official |
| 65 | sharding | database sharding, shards | "Sharding is the process of distributing a large database across multiple servers. Each server contains a subset of the data, and queries are distributed across all servers" / "different possible approaches to horizontal partitioning, including hash-based, range-based, and round-robin sharding" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shard_(database_architecture) | non-official | 分片 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/database-sharding/ | AWS official |
| 66 | replication | database replication, replicas | "Replication is the process of copying data from one database server to another. Every node that stores a copy of the data is called a replica" / "By maintaining multiple copies of data across different host machines, replication provides the following benefits" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Replication_(computing) | non-official | 复制 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/USER_ReadRepl.html | AWS official |
| 67 | single-leader replication | leader-follower replication, master-slave replication, 单主复制 | "In single-leader replication, one database server serves as the leader, and the other servers serve as followers. The leader server handles all write operations while the follower servers handle read operations" / "This type of replication is useful for scaling read-heavy databases" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Replication_(computing) | non-official | 单领导者复制 | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/518312160 | official (other source) |
| 68 | multileader replication | multi-master replication, 多主复制 | "In multileader replication, each database server can both read and write data. Changes made to one server are replicated to the other server either synchronously or asynchronously" / "This type of replication is useful for scaling databases that require high availability" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Replication_(computing) | non-official | 多领导者复制 | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/518312160 | official (other source) |
| 69 | synchronous replication | 2-safe replication | "synchronous replication provides a mechanism for replicating data from a leader replica to follower replicas using synchronous communication, keeping data always up to date across all replicas" / "the client considers a write operation successful only when it is acknowledged by both the leader replica and the synchronous follower replica" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Replication_(computing) | non-official | 同步复制 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/USER_ReadRepl.html | AWS official |
| 70 | asynchronous replication | — | "asynchronous replication provides a mechanism for replicating data from a leader replica to asynchronous replicas" / "asynchronous replication is often favored in distributed systems that prioritize scalability and performance over strict consistency" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Replication_(computing) | non-official | 异步复制 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/USER_ReadRepl.html | AWS official |
| 71 | high availability | HA | "Replication ensures high availability by storing copies of data on multiple host machines. In the event of a failure or downtime of one host machine, the database can seamlessly redirect read and write operations to other live machines" / "This fault-tolerance mechanism prevents service disruptions and ensures continuous access to data" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | non-official | 高可用性 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/AuroraUserGuide/Aurora.Replication.html | AWS official |
| 72 | load distribution | load balancing | "With replication, the database can distribute read and write queries across multiple host machines. This load-distribution strategy prevents overburdening of individual machines, thereby improving overall system performance and scalability" / "replication enables efficient utilization of computing resources and better handling of concurrent user requests" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 负载均衡 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_BestPractices.html | AWS official |
| 73 | disaster recovery | DR | "Replication serves as a foundation for disaster-recovery strategies. By maintaining multiple copies of data, distributed databases can withstand catastrophic events, hardware failures, or natural disasters" / "Replication ensures data resilience and minimizes the risk of data loss" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | non-official | 灾难恢复 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/USER_ReplicateBackups.html | AWS official |
| 74 | resiliency | data resilience | "Replication ensures data resilience and minimizes the risk of data loss, contributing to the overall reliability and robustness of the database" / "In case of data loss or unavailability on one host machine, the replicated copies can be used for data recovery and system restoration" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | non-official | 弹性 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_Storage.html | AWS official |
| 75 | scalability | horizontal scaling, scale out, scale up | "Replication plays a crucial role in scaling distributed databases. As the data size or user load increases, additional host machines can be added to the system" / "This horizontal-scaling approach allows the database to handle larger workloads and accommodate growing user demands" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | non-official | 可扩展性 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_BestPractices.html | AWS official |
| 76 | latency | network latency | "Replicating data across geographically distributed host machines allows for placing the replicated copies closer to end users. This proximity reduces the network latency experienced by users when accessing the database" / "By minimizing the distance between the data and the user, replication improves response times and enhances the user experience" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | non-official | 延迟 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/CHAP_BestPractices.html | AWS official |
| 77 | MySQL | — | "MySQL is an open source RDBMS that was first released in 1995. It is one of the most popular RDBMSs in the world, with a large user community and a broad range of features" / "MySQL is known for its speed and scalability, making it an ideal choice for businesses that need to handle high volumes of data and traffic" | https://dev.mysql.com/doc | non-official | MySQL | https://dev.mysql.com/doc/refman/8.0/en/ | non-official |
| 78 | PostgreSQL | — | "PostgreSQL is an open source RDBMS that was first released in 1996. It is known for its robustness, reliability, and feature-richness" / "PostgreSQL supports query plans that can leverage multiple CPUs to answer queries with greater speed" | https://www.postgresql.org/docs | non-official | PostgreSQL | https://www.postgresql.org/docs/ | non-official |

# Glossary: ch04 — Caching Policies and Strategies

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch04.html`.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 79 | cache | caching, cached data | "a cache is a component or mechanism used to store frequently accessed data or instructions closer to the computing unit, reducing the latency of retrieving the information from slower storage or external resources" / "Caches play a crucial role in improving system performance and reducing latency" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 缓存 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/elasticache/ | AWS official |
| 80 | locality | principle of locality, temporal locality, spatial locality | "The concept of caching revolves around the principle of locality, which suggests that programs tend to access a relatively small portion of their data or instructions repeatedly" / "By storing this frequently accessed information in a cache, subsequent access to the same data can be served faster" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Locality_of_reference | non-official | 局部性原理 | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/352325821 | non-official |
| 81 | cache hit | — | "A cache hit occurs when the requested data is found in the cache, allowing for fast retrieval without accessing the slower main memory or external resources" / "Cache hit rates measure the effectiveness of the cache in serving requests without needing to access slower external storage" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 缓存命中 | https://blog.csdn.net/weixin_44259720/article/details/122995882 | non-official |
| 82 | cache miss | — | "A cache miss happens when the requested data is not present in the cache, requiring the system to fetch the data from the main memory or external storage" / "cache miss rates indicate how often the cache fails to serve requested data" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 缓存未命中 | https://blog.csdn.net/weixin_44259720/article/details/122995882 | non-official |
| 83 | cache hit ratio | cache hit rate | "Cache eviction policies try to maximize the cache hit ratio: the percentage of time the requested item was found in the cache and served" / "A higher cache hit ratio reduces the need to retrieve data from external storage, resulting in better system performance" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 缓存命中率 | https://blog.csdn.net/weixin_44259720/article/details/122995882 | non-official |
| 84 | Amdahl's law | — | "Amdahl's law states that the overall speed increase achieved by optimizing a particular component of a system is limited by the fraction of time that component is utilized" / "Amdahl's law emphasizes the importance of efficient caching to maximize the benefits of performance optimization" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amdahl%27s_law | non-official | 阿姆达尔定律 | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/48022905 | non-official |
| 85 | Pareto distribution | 80/20 rule | "The Pareto distribution, also known as the 80/20 rule, states that a significant portion of the system's workload is driven by a small fraction of the data" / "Caching aligns well with this distribution by allowing the frequently accessed data to reside in a fast cache" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pareto_distribution | non-official | 帕累托分布（二八定律） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/183101311 | non-official |
| 86 | cache eviction policy | cache eviction, eviction policies | "Caching policies determine how the cache handles data eviction and replacement when its capacity is reached" / "Cache eviction policies try to maximize the cache hit ratio: the percentage of time the requested item was found in the cache and served" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_replacement_policies | non-official | 缓存淘汰策略 | https://blog.csdn.net/weixin_44259720/article/details/122995882 | non-official |
| 87 | Belady's algorithm | — | "Belady's algorithm is an optimal caching algorithm that evicts the data item that will be used farthest in the future" / "Belady's algorithm serves as a theoretical benchmark for evaluating the performance of other caching policies" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Page_replacement_algorithm | non-official | Belady算法（最优置换算法） | https://blog.csdn.net/qq_36268103/article/details/142587005 | non-official |
| 88 | FIFO | first in, first out | "FIFO is a simple caching policy that evicts the oldest data item from the cache. It follows the principle that the first data item inserted into the cache is the first one to be evicted when the cache is full" / "This can lead to premature eviction of items that are still in demand, resulting in the aging problem" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFO_(computing_and_electronics) | non-official | 先进先出（FIFO） | https://blog.csdn.net/weixin_44259720/article/details/122995882 | non-official |
| 89 | LIFO | last in, first out | "LIFO is the opposite of FIFO, where the most recently inserted data item is the first one to be evicted" / "LIFO does not consider the access pattern and can result in poor cache utilization and eviction decisions" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stack_(abstract_data_type) | non-official | 后进先出（LIFO） | https://blog.csdn.net/weixin_44259720/article/details/122995882 | non-official |
| 90 | LRU | least recently used | "LRU is a popular caching policy that evicts the least recently accessed data item from the cache. It assumes that recently accessed items are more likely to be accessed in the near future" / "LRU requires tracking access timestamps for each item, making it slightly more complex to implement" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_replacement_policies | non-official | 最近最少使用（LRU） | https://blog.csdn.net/weixin_44259720/article/details/122995882 | non-official |
| 91 | MRU | most recently used | "MRU evicts the most recently accessed data item from the cache. It assumes that the most recently accessed item is unlikely to be accessed again soon" / "MRU is effective in scenarios where a small subset of items is frequently accessed and those older items are more likely to be reused" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_replacement_policies | non-official | 最近最常使用（MRU） | https://blog.csdn.net/moakun/article/details/80075608 | non-official |
| 92 | LFU | least frequently used | "LFU evicts the least frequently accessed data item from the cache. It assumes that items with lower access frequency are less likely to be accessed in the future" / "LFU requires maintaining access frequency counts for each item, which can be memory intensive" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_replacement_policies | non-official | 最不经常使用（LFU） | https://blog.csdn.net/weixin_44259720/article/details/122995882 | non-official |
| 93 | LFRU | least frequently recently used | "LFRU combines the concepts of LFU and LRU by considering both the frequency of access and the recency of access. It evicts the item with the lowest frequency count among the least recently used items" / "In CDNs that serve frequently accessed static content, LFRU can optimize the cache by balancing recency and frequency" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_replacement_policies | non-official | 最不经常最近使用（LFRU） | https://blog.csdn.net/qq_36268103/article/details/142587005 | non-official |
| 94 | allowlist policy | allowlist | "An allowlist policy for cache replacement is a mechanism that defines a set of prioritized items eligible for retention in a cache when space is limited" / "an allowlist policy focuses on explicitly specifying which items should be preserved in the cache" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitelist | non-official | 允许列表策略 | https://blog.csdn.net/Anne1997/article/details/132120084 | non-official |
| 95 | cache invalidation | invalidation policies | "Cache invalidation is a crucial aspect of cache management that ensures the cached data remains consistent with the underlying data source" / "Effective cache invalidation strategies help maintain data integrity and prevent stale or outdated data from being served" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_invalidation | non-official | 缓存失效 | https://blog.csdn.net/pbrlovejava/article/details/125953394 | non-official |
| 96 | active invalidation | active cache invalidation | "Active invalidation involves explicitly removing or invalidating cached data when changes occur in the underlying data source" / "Active invalidation provides precise control over cache consistency but requires additional overhead to manage the invalidation process effectively" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_invalidation | non-official | 主动失效（主动缓存清除） | https://blog.csdn.net/pbrlovejava/article/details/125953394 | non-official |
| 97 | ETag | entity tag | "An ETag is a unique identifier assigned to a specific version of a resource on a web server and used for cache validation" / "When a client requests a resource, it can include the ETag in an If-None-Match header to check if the cached version is still valid" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HTTP_ETag | non-official | 实体标签（ETag） | https://developer.mozilla.org/zh-CN/docs/Web/HTTP/Headers/ETag | official (other source) |
| 98 | TTL | time to live | "TTL is a cache invalidation technique that associates a time duration with each cached item" / "After the TTL period elapses, the cache treats the item as expired, and subsequent requests for the expired item trigger cache misses" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 生存时间（TTL） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/Expiration.html | AWS official |
| 99 | cache-aside | lazy loading | "The cache-aside caching strategy, also known as lazy loading, delegates the responsibility of managing the cache to the application code" / "Cache-aside caching offers flexibility because the application has full control over caching decisions" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 旁路缓存（Cache-Aside） | https://blog.csdn.net/weixin_42002747/article/details/109016611 | non-official |
| 100 | read-through | read-through caching strategy | "The read-through caching strategy retrieves data from the cache if available; otherwise, the cache itself fetches the data from the underlying data source" / "Unlike the cache-aside strategy, this strategy offloads the responsibility of managing cache lookups from the application" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 读穿透（Read-Through） | https://blog.csdn.net/lzx_victory/article/details/107848162 | non-official |
| 101 | refresh-ahead | prefetching | "The refresh-ahead caching strategy, also known as prefetching, proactively retrieves data from the data source into the cache before it is explicitly requested" / "By prefetching data, the cache reduces latency for subsequent read requests and improves the overall performance of data retrieval" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 预刷新（Refresh-Ahead） | https://blog.csdn.net/lzx_victory/article/details/107848162 | non-official |
| 102 | cache warm-up | warm-up | "Cache warm-up involves preloading data into the cache before it is needed, ensuring that frequently accessed data is readily available" / "a warm start involves having data preloaded in the cache, allowing subsequent requests to be served quickly from memory" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 缓存预热 | https://blog.csdn.net/T_110140/article/details/125000918 | non-official |
| 103 | cold start | — | "A cold start occurs when the cache is empty, requiring the system to fetch data from the underlying source, which leads to higher latency and potential performance bottlenecks as the cache fills" / "read-through caching can initially introduce higher latency during the warm-up period due to cache misses" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cold_start_(computing) | non-official | 冷启动 | https://blog.csdn.net/T_110140/article/details/125000918 | non-official |
| 104 | write-through | write-through caching strategy | "The write-through caching strategy involves writing data to both the cache and the underlying data source simultaneously" / "This strategy ensures that the data remains consistent between the cache and the data source" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 写穿透（Write-Through） | https://blog.csdn.net/lzx_victory/article/details/107848162 | non-official |
| 105 | write-around | write-around caching strategy | "The write-around caching strategy involves bypassing the cache for write operations. When the application wants to update data, it writes directly to the underlying data source, bypassing the cache" / "the written data does not reside in the cache, reducing cache pollution with infrequently accessed data" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 绕写（Write-Around） | https://blog.csdn.net/ycx1225/article/details/103032330 | non-official |
| 106 | write-back | write-back caching strategy | "The write-back caching strategy allows write operations to be performed directly on the cache, deferring the update to the underlying data source until a later time" / "Write-back caching provides faster write operations by reducing the number of immediate disk writes" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 写回（Write-Back） | https://blog.csdn.net/lzx_victory/article/details/107848162 | non-official |
| 107 | in-process caching | — | "The cache resides within the same process or application as the requesting component. It is typically implemented as an in-memory data store and is directly accessible by the application or service" / "In-process caching provides fast data access and low latency" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 进程内缓存 | https://blog.csdn.net/pbrlovejava/article/details/125953394 | non-official |
| 108 | interprocess caching | — | "The cache is deployed as a separate process or service that runs alongside the applications or services. It acts as a dedicated caching layer that can be accessed by multiple applications or processes" / "Interprocess caching improves resource utilization and data consistency across different components" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-process_communication | non-official | 进程间缓存 | https://blog.csdn.net/pbrlovejava/article/details/125953394 | non-official |
| 109 | remote caching | — | "The cache is deployed as a separate service or cluster that runs on a different machine or location than the requesting components" / "Remote caching can be used to share cached data among different applications or services running on separate machines or even across geographically distributed locations" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 远程缓存 | https://blog.csdn.net/pbrlovejava/article/details/125953394 | non-official |
| 110 | client-side caching | — | "Cached data is stored on the client device, typically in the browser's memory or local storage. This mechanism allows web applications to store and retrieve static resources, such as HTML, CSS, JavaScript, and images, directly from the client's device" / "Client-side caching reduces the need to fetch resources from the server on subsequent requests" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 客户端缓存 | https://blog.csdn.net/pbrlovejava/article/details/125953394 | non-official |
| 111 | content delivery network | CDN, CDNs | "CDNs play a crucial role in improving the performance and availability of web content for end users by caching at edge locations to reduce latency and enhance scalability" / "Static content is cached on distributed servers that are strategically located across different geographic regions" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 内容分发网络（CDN） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/cloudfront/ | AWS official |
| 112 | web server caching | — | "These caching mechanisms are implemented at the server side to store frequently accessed content. When a request is made to the server, it first checks if the requested content is already cached" / "Web server caching is effective for static web pages, dynamic content with a long expiration time, and content that is expensive to generate" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_cache | non-official | Web服务器缓存 | https://blog.csdn.net/pbrlovejava/article/details/125953394 | non-official |
| 113 | application caching | — | "Data is cached within the application's memory or in-memory databases. This mechanism is typically used to store frequently accessed data or computation results that are costly to generate or retrieve from other data sources" / "Application caching improves response times by reducing the need for repeated data retrieval and computation" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 应用程序缓存 | https://blog.csdn.net/pbrlovejava/article/details/125953394 | non-official |
| 114 | database caching | — | "Database caching focuses on improving the performance of database operations by caching frequently accessed data or query results" / "This mechanism can be implemented at two levels: query-level caching and object-level caching" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Database_caching | non-official | 数据库缓存 | https://blog.csdn.net/pbrlovejava/article/details/125953394 | non-official |
| 115 | query-level caching | — | "Query-level caching stores the results of frequently executed queries in memory. When the same query is executed, the cached result is served instead of querying the database again" / "reducing database load and improving response times" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 查询级缓存 | https://blog.csdn.net/pbrlovejava/article/details/125953394 | non-official |
| 116 | object-level caching | — | "Object-level caching caches individual data objects or records retrieved from the database. This mechanism is useful when accessing specific objects frequently or when the database is relatively static" / "Object-level caching reduces the need for frequent database queries, improving overall application performance" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cache_(computing) | non-official | 对象级缓存 | https://blog.csdn.net/pbrlovejava/article/details/125953394 | non-official |
| 117 | push CDN | — | "In a push CDN, content is precached and distributed to edge servers in advance. The CDN provider proactively pushes content to edge locations based on predicted demand or predetermined rules" / "Push CDNs are suitable for websites with low traffic or content that doesn't require frequent updates" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Content_delivery_network | non-official | 推送型CDN | https://blog.csdn.net/maijian/article/details/21168541 | non-official |
| 118 | pull CDN | — | "In a pull CDN, content is cached on demand. The CDN servers pull the content from the origin server when the first user requests it" / "Pull CDNs are well suited for websites with high traffic since recently requested content remains on the CDN, evenly distributing the load" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Content_delivery_network | non-official | 拉取型CDN | https://blog.csdn.net/maijian/article/details/21168541 | non-official |
| 119 | content fragmentation | — | "Breaking down dynamic content into smaller fragments to enable partial caching and efficient updates" / "CDNs face challenges when caching dynamic content that frequently changes based on user interactions or real-time data" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Content_delivery_network | non-official | 内容分片 | https://blog.csdn.net/maijian/article/details/21168541 | non-official |
| 120 | Edge Side Includes | ESI, ESI tags | "Implementing ESI tags to separate dynamic and static content, allowing dynamic portions to be processed on the fly while caching the static fragments" / "CDNs employ various techniques: content fragmentation, Edge Side Includes (ESI), content personalization" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edge_Side_Includes | non-official | 边缘端包含（ESI） | https://blog.csdn.net/maijian/article/details/21168541 | non-official |
| 121 | content personalization | — | "Leveraging user profiling and segmentation techniques to cache personalized or user-specific content at the edge servers" / "CDNs employ various techniques to optimize dynamic content caching" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Personalization | non-official | 内容个性化 | https://blog.csdn.net/alitech2017/article/details/106383383 | non-official |
| 122 | multitier CDN architecture | — | "Multitier CDN architecture involves distributing content across multiple layers or tiers of edge servers. This approach allows for better scalability, fault tolerance, and improved content delivery to geographically diverse regions" / "It enables efficient content replication and reduces latency by bringing content closer to end users" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Content_delivery_network | non-official | 多层CDN架构 | https://blog.csdn.net/maijian/article/details/21168541 | non-official |
| 123 | DNS redirection | — | "CDNs use DNS redirection to direct user requests to the nearest or most suitable edge server based on factors like geographic proximity, network conditions, and server availability" / "optimizing content delivery and reducing latency" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DNS_hijacking | non-official | DNS重定向 | https://blog.csdn.net/maijian/article/details/21168541 | non-official |
| 124 | client multiplexing | — | "This technique combines multiple HTTP requests and responses into a single connection between the client and the edge server. This reduces the overhead of establishing multiple connections and improves efficiency" / "especially for small object requests, resulting in faster content delivery" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiplexing | non-official | 客户端多路复用 | https://blog.csdn.net/maijian/article/details/21168541 | non-official |
| 125 | Amazon CloudFront | CloudFront | "AWS offers Amazon CloudFront, a pull CDN built for high performance, security, and developer convenience" / "CDN caching: Static content is cached on distributed servers that are strategically located across different geographic regions" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 亚马逊 CloudFront | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/cloudfront/ | AWS official |
| 126 | Memcached | — | "Memcached is an open source, high-performance caching solution widely used in web applications. It operates as a distributed memory object caching system, storing data in memory across multiple servers" / "Cache-aside is the strategy most used with Memcached, allowing applications to manage caching behavior based on usage patterns" | https://aws.amazon.com/elasticache/memcached/ | official | Memcached | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/elasticache/ | AWS official |
| 127 | slab allocation | slabs, pages | "Memcached employs a slab allocation strategy. It divides the allocated memory into fixed-size chunks called slabs. Each slab is further divided into smaller units known as pages" / "The slab allocation strategy allows Memcached to efficiently manage memory by grouping items of similar sizes together" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slab_allocation | non-official | slab分配（分块分配） | https://blog.csdn.net/xzengwei1313/article/details/83376219 | non-official |
| 128 | Redis | Remote Dictionary Server | "Redis, short for Remote Dictionary Server, is a server-based, in-memory data structure store that can serve as a high-performance cache" / "Redis organizes data in customizable data structures from the ground up, supporting a wide range of data types, such as strings, bitmaps, bitfields, lists, sets, hashes, geospatial, and hyperlog" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | Redis | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/elasticache/ | AWS official |
| 129 | pub/sub | publish-subscribe, pub/sub messaging | "Redis includes publish-subscribe (pub/sub) messaging capabilities, enabling real-time messaging and event-driven architectures" / "This makes it suitable for scenarios involving real-time data updates and notifications" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Publish%E2%80%93subscribe_pattern | non-official | 发布/订阅（Pub/Sub） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/177000194 | non-official |
| 130 | Redis Sentinel | — | "Redis Sentinel is a distributed system that ensures high availability for Redis. Sentinel processes coordinate the state and monitor the availability of main and secondary instances" / "Sentinel processes can start a failover process if the primary instance becomes unavailable" | https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/sentinel/ | non-official | Redis哨兵（Redis Sentinel） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/177000194 | non-official |
| 131 | Redis Cluster | — | "Redis Cluster enables horizontal scaling by distributing data across multiple machines or shards. Algorithmic sharding is used to determine which Redis instance (shard) holds a specific key" / "Redis Cluster employs a hash-slotting mechanism to map data to shards and allows for seamless resharding when adding new instances" | https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/scaling/ | non-official | Redis集群（Redis Cluster） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/463455831 | non-official |
| 132 | RDB files | Redis database files, snapshot-based persistence | "RDB is the default persistence model in Redis. It periodically creates snapshots of the dataset and saves them as binary RDB files" / "RDB files are highly efficient in terms of disk space usage and data loading speed" | https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/persistence/ | non-official | RDB文件（快照文件） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/340082703 | non-official |
| 133 | AOF | append-only files, append-only file persistence | "AOF persistence is an alternative persistence model to RDB files that logs every write operation to an append-only file" / "AOF captures a sequential log of write operations, enabling Redis to reconstruct the dataset by replaying the log" | https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/persistence/ | non-official | 仅追加文件（AOF） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/340082703 | non-official |
| 134 | write-ahead log | WAL | "AOF persists every write operation to the append-only file as a series of commands or raw data. This log can be used to rebuild the dataset from scratch" / "AOF offers more durability than RDB files since it captures every write operation" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Write-ahead_logging | non-official | 预写日志（WAL） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/340082703 | non-official |
| 135 | copy-on-write | CoW | "Redis leverages forking and copy-on-write (CoW) techniques to facilitate data persistence efficiently within its single-threaded architecture" / "when either the parent or the child process modifies a memory page, CoW comes into play. Instead of immediately duplicating the modified page, the operating system creates a new copy only when necessary" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copy-on-write | non-official | 写时复制（CoW） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/452676926 | non-official |
| 136 | forking | fork(), fork-based persistence | "Redis uses the fork() system call to create a child process, which is an identical copy of the parent process. Forking is a lightweight operation as it creates a CoW clone of the parent's memory" / "Redis uses fork-based persistence to avoid blocking the main event loop during the snapshot process" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fork_(system_call) | non-official | 进程分叉（fork） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/588732172 | non-official |
| 137 | Amazon ElastiCache | ElastiCache | "AWS offers Amazon ElastiCache, which is compatible with both Redis and Memcached for real-time use cases like caching, session stores, gaming, geospatial services, analytics, and queuing" / "two widely adopted open source caching solutions: Memcached and Redis" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 亚马逊 ElastiCache | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/elasticache/ | AWS official |
| 138 | Amazon MemoryDB | MemoryDB | "AWS has introduced Amazon MemoryDB, a Redis-compatible, durable, in-memory database service designed for applications requiring microsecond read and single-digit millisecond write performance" / "With a focus on data durability and high availability, MemoryDB is ideal for use cases like caching, session management, and real-time analytics" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/memorydb/latest/devguide/what-is-memorydb.html | official | 亚马逊 MemoryDB（亚马逊内存数据库） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/memorydb/latest/devguide/what-is-memorydb.html | AWS official |
| 139 | interprocess communication | IPC | "Applications communicate with the cache using interprocess communication mechanisms such as shared memory, pipes, sockets, or remote procedure calls (RPCs)" / "Interprocess caching introduces moderate latency due to the need for interprocess communication (IPC) mechanisms" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-process_communication | non-official | 进程间通信（IPC） | https://blog.csdn.net/xbwueric3/article/details/79325706 | non-official |
| 140 | gossip protocol | — | "Gossip Protocol is used in Redis Cluster to maintain cluster health. Nodes constantly communicate to determine the availability of shards and can promote secondary instances to primary if needed" / "Redis Cluster enables horizontal scaling by distributing data across multiple machines or shards" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gossip_protocol | non-official | Gossip协议（流言协议） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/463455831 | non-official |
| 141 | hash slotting | hash slot, hash-slotting mechanism | "Redis Cluster employs a hash-slotting mechanism to map data to shards and allows for seamless resharding when adding new instances to the cluster" / "Algorithmic sharding is used to determine which Redis instance (shard) holds a specific key" | https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/scaling/ | non-official | 哈希槽分区（哈希槽） | https://blog.csdn.net/hudeyong926/article/details/120950525 | non-official |

# Glossary: ch06 — Communication Networks and Protocols

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch06.html`.

> **Note on ch06 terms:** This chapter covers internet communication protocols (OSI, TCP/IP, HTTP, SMTP, MQTT, WebRTC, etc.) and API styles (REST, GraphQL, RPC, SOAP). Most terms are general networking/computing concepts with widely accepted Chinese translations sourced from zh.wikipedia.org or CSDN. AWS-specific terms (MQTT, QoS, WebSocket) are confirmed from AWS Chinese documentation at docs.amazonaws.cn. Service names AWS Shield and AWS AppSync have no Chinese translation and are kept in English per AWS convention. GraphQL is also kept in English as no standard Chinese name exists.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 142 | OSI model | Open Systems Interconnection (OSI) model, OSI model layers, application layer (L7), physical layer (L1) | "The OSI model is a reference model that divides the communication network into seven layers, with each layer responsible for performing a specific job" / "In modern-age internet applications, we don't see an OSI model being practically present; it serves only as a reference communication model" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSI_model | non-official | OSI模型（开放系统互联模型） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/OSI%E6%A8%A1%E5%9E%8B | non-official |
| 143 | TCP/IP model | internet protocol suite | "The TCP/IP model, also referred to as the internet protocol suite, mainly includes TCP, IP, and UDP" / "The TCP/IP model combines multiple layers of the OSI model into one layer to execute similar functionality" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_protocol_suite | non-official | TCP/IP模型（互联网协议套件） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/TCP/IP%E5%8D%8F%E8%AE%AE%E6%97%8F | non-official |
| 144 | Internet Protocol (IP) | IP (Internet Protocol), IP address | "The most used protocol in the network/internet layer is the Internet Protocol (IP). It is a set of rules responsible for delivering data packets from a source IP address to a destination IP address" / "The IP address uniquely identifies the source and destination of data packets across the network and helps routers to route this information from one place to another" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_Protocol | non-official | 互联网协议（IP） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E7%BD%91%E9%99%85%E5%8D%8F%E8%AE%AE | non-official |
| 145 | IP datagram | IP datagrams, payload | "The payload consists of actual data (called IP datagrams) that is to be transferred over the network" / "Each packet in IP protocol contains an IP header and payload. The IP header has the source IP address and the destination IP address, along with any other metadata (such as IP version, packet size, and TTL) required to route the packet" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IPv4#Datagram_structure | non-official | IP数据报 | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/IPv4 | non-official |
| 146 | fragmentation | IP fragmentation, data packets | "Data across the internet is often broken into chunks (referred to as fragmentation) to avoid the burden of delivering large packets and to reduce latency" / "L4 breaks down the data into smaller chunks, called data packets, before passing it on to L3" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IP_fragmentation | non-official | 分片（IP分片） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/IP%E5%88%86%E7%89%87 | non-official |
| 147 | maximum transmission unit (MTU) | MTU | "There is also a limit to the maximum size of a packet (1,500 bytes for ethernet) that can be transmitted over the network interface: the maximum transmission unit (MTU)" / "Data across the internet is often broken into chunks (referred to as fragmentation) to avoid the burden of delivering large packets and to reduce latency" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximum_transmission_unit | non-official | 最大传输单元（MTU） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E6%9C%80%E5%A4%A7%E4%BC%A0%E8%BE%93%E5%8D%95%E5%85%83 | non-official |
| 148 | Internet Control Message Protocol (ICMP) | ICMP | "Another protocol that operates on this layer is the Internet Control Message Protocol (ICMP). This protocol is generally used for network diagnostics and implementing error mechanisms" / "If no, the receiver responds with an ICMP packet mentioning that the destination is not reachable" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_Control_Message_Protocol | non-official | 互联网控制消息协议（ICMP） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E4%BA%92%E8%81%94%E7%BD%91%E6%8E%A7%E5%88%B6%E6%B6%88%E6%81%AF%E5%8D%8F%E8%AE%AE | non-official |
| 149 | Transmission Control Protocol (TCP) | TCP | "The Transmission Control Protocol (TCP) is a transport layer protocol that offers reliable communication between sender and receiver with the capability of retransmitting data in case of packet loss" / "Communication via TCP is initiated between the sender and receiver via a three-way handshake process. The three-way handshake process is an agreement between the two parties to send and accept the data" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transmission_Control_Protocol | non-official | 传输控制协议（TCP） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E4%BC%A0%E8%BE%93%E6%8E%A7%E5%88%B6%E5%8D%8F%E8%AE%AE | non-official |
| 150 | three-way handshake | handshake process, TCP handshake | "Communication via TCP is initiated between the sender and receiver via a three-way handshake process. The three-way handshake process is an agreement between the two parties to send and accept the data" / "It is faster because it doesn't involve a three-way handshake process for establishing a connection, and it provides no guarantee of data delivery, so there is no overhead of retries" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handshaking | non-official | 三次握手 | https://blog.csdn.net/BoilerUp/article/details/109711915 | non-official |
| 151 | slow start | — | "The data transfer begins with a small data size (one segment in a congestion window)—hence the term slow start—and the sender gradually increases the window size in an exponential manner (the window is increased by one segment on every ACK)" / "The maximum allowed data transfer size depends on the advertised window size by the receiver" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TCP_congestion_control#Slow_start | non-official | 慢启动 | https://blog.csdn.net/jtracydy/article/details/52366461 | non-official |
| 152 | congestion avoidance | congestion control, congestion window | "Congestion is detected if there are connection timeouts or if duplicate ACKs are received. | 153 | port | well-known ports, registered ports, ephemeral ports, system ports, user ports, dynamic ports, nonephemeral ports | "A port is a virtual point managed by an OS that defines an entry to or exit from a software application (numbered from 0 to 65535)" / "Each port is responsible for certain types of network communication, and the role is assigned based on the range it falls into" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_(computer_networking) | non-official | 端口（知名端口、注册端口、临时端口） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E9%80%9A%E8%A8%8A%E5%9F%A0 | non-official |
| 154 | User Datagram Protocol (UDP) | UDP | "The UDP transport protocol is less reliable but faster than TCP and works for these use cases. | 159 | Simple Mail Transfer Protocol (SMTP) | SMTP, SMTP server, SMTP client, relay server, mail transfer agents (MTAs), gateway | "The SMTP protocol is used for email communications between two or more parties" / "SMTP essentially operates on a push mechanism, transferring email contents from clients to servers" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simple_Mail_Transfer_Protocol | non-official | 简单邮件传输协议（SMTP） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E7%AE%80%E5%8D%95%E9%82%AE%E4%BB%B6%E4%BC%A0%E8%BE%93%E5%8D%8F%E8%AE%AE | non-official |

| 165 | quality of service (QoS) levels | QoS, QoS Level 0, QoS Level 1, QoS Level 2 | "MQTT supports bidirectional communications between the devices and the cloud and offers multiple quality of service (QoS) levels for ensuring message delivery guarantees" / "The three QoS levels supported by MQTT are: At most once (QoS Level 0), At least once (QoS Level 1), Exactly once (QoS Level 2)" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MQTT#Quality_of_service | non-official | 服务质量（QoS）级别 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/iot/latest/developerguide/mqtt.html | AWS official |
| 166 | synchronous communication | sync communication, sync | "In synchronous communication between two parties, the first party requests some data and then waits for the response from the second party" / "Sync communications are generally preferred for low-latency calls: we're aware that the server will return the response for the corresponding request within the expected time frame and won't take too long" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synchronous_serial_communication | non-official | 同步通信 | https://blog.csdn.net/u014532901/article/details/82079443 | non-official |
| 167 | asynchronous communication | async communication, async | "In the case of asynchronous communication, the first party requests some data but doesn't wait for the response. | 168 | HTTP polling | HTTP regular polling, HTTP long polling, pull-based mechanism | "Clients ask the server for a recent status at regular intervals, such as five seconds. | 169 | WebSockets | WebSocket, persistent connection, full-duplex | "WebSockets represent a bidirectional persistent connection (full-duplex communication on a single TCP connection) over HTTP where clients and servers communicate with each other. Two people (or more, in the case of user groups) interact with each other in real time, so a persistent connection makes more sense here" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WebSocket | non-official | WebSocket（持久连接） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/apigateway/latest/developerguide/apigateway-websocket-api-overview.html | AWS official |
| 170 | server-sent events (SSEs) | SSEs, SSE, EventSource, push-based mechanism | "SSE is implemented over a long-lived HTTP connection to consume any updates or notifications from the server in text format.
