| 171 | Remote Procedure Call (RPC) | RPC, gRPC, Thrift, procedures | "RPC means executing this piece of code, aka the procedure, on a remote machine as if it were being executed on your local personal computer or laptop" / "RPC helps with cross-language and platform support by taking on the headache of data marshaling (encoding) and unmarshaling (decoding)" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remote_procedure_call | non-official | 远程过程调用（RPC） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E9%81%A0%E7%A8%8B%E9%81%8E%E7%A8%8B%E8%AA%BF%E7%94%A8 | non-official |
| 172 | stub function | stub, client stub, server stub | "A stub function offers a similar interface as a remote method that clients can invoke, and it internally handles the implementation details, such as network communication and marshaling of method arguments to a message over the network" / "On the server side, the stub function will unmarshal the message to the arguments as expected by the remote method" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stub_(distributed_computing) | non-official | 存根函数（存根） | https://blog.csdn.net/daaikuaichuan/article/details/88595202 | non-official |
| 173 | interface definition language (IDL) | IDL, WSDL, Apache Thrift IDL | "The common interface or a contract to specify the remote procedures and data types is defined in the interface definition language (IDL). This interface is language agnostic, meaning it will work with any desired programming language" / "Some examples include the Web Services Description Language (WSDL), which is used with Simple Object Access Protocol (SOAP), and the Apache Thrift IDL" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interface_description_language | non-official | 接口定义语言（IDL） | https://blog.csdn.net/chszs/article/details/2667170 | non-official |
| 174 | SOAP (Simple Object Access Protocol) | SOAP | "SOAP is a protocol that offers mechanisms to clients and servers for communicating with one another using XML messages" / "SOAP is not tied to any specific protocol and can be used alongside other protocols, such as HTTP, SMTP, and TCP, although it is mostly used with HTTP" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SOAP | non-official | 简单对象访问协议（SOAP） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/SOAP | non-official |
| 175 | REST (Representational State Transfer) | REST, RESTful, RESTful APIs | "REST is widely used alongside the HTTP protocol for client-server interactions and uses HTTP methods to implement different CRUD (create, read, update, and delete) operations. REST is a software architectural style, not a protocol like SOAP, and is used extensively for building applications for today's internet" / "Note that the APIs following these standards are referred to as RESTful APIs" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/REST | non-official | 表述性状态转移（REST） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E8%A1%A8%E7%8E%B0%E5%B1%82%E7%8A%B6%E6%80%81%E8%BD%AC%E6%8D%A2 | non-official |
| 176 | GraphQL | GraphQL query, GraphQL Schema Definition Language (SDL), mutations, GraphQL subscriptions | "GraphQL is an API query language that allows clients to write a query specific to data retrieved on a single API endpoint (unlike REST, where we define endpoints per API)" / "There can be scenarios of overfetching or underfetching data with REST, which can be resolved by the query flexibility offered by GraphQL" | https://graphql.org/learn | non-official | GraphQL | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/appsync/latest/devguide/what-is-appsync.html | AWS official |
| 177 | AWS AppSync | AppSync | "One of the benefits of GraphQL over REST is its integration with a single endpoint and the fact that it serves all the API needs with the same API endpoint, instead of multiple endpoints" / "choosing a GraphQL client like Apollo or AWS AppSync to help applications scale with much more ease" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/appsync/latest/devguide/what-is-appsync.html | official | AWS AppSync | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/appsync/ | AWS official |
| 178 | WebRTC (Web Real-Time Communication) | WebRTC, RTCPeerConnection, RTCDataChannel | "WebRTC (which stands for Web Real-Time Communication) offers a mechanism for supporting audio and video communications in web browsers or mobile applications by APIs without requiring installation of any specific plug-ins" / "WebRTC allows direct peer-to-peer connection without any requirement for a server in between" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WebRTC | non-official | 网页实时通信（WebRTC） | https://cloud.baidu.com/article/3319610 | non-official |
| 179 | Interactive Connectivity Establishment (ICE) | ICE, ICE candidates | "WebRTC can use Interactive Connectivity Establishment (ICE), which helps figure out the optimal communication route" / "The two peer devices exchange ICE candidates (network address and port) with each other via a signaling mechanism such as SDP" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interactive_Connectivity_Establishment | non-official | 交互式连接建立（ICE） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E4%BA%92%E5%8B%95%E5%BC%8F%E9%80%A3%E6%8E%A5%E5%BB%BA%E7%AB%8B | non-official |
| 180 | Session Traversal Utilities for NAT (STUN) protocol | STUN, STUN server | "The Session Traversal Utilities for NAT (STUN) protocol is used if the devices are behind a NAT. Both the peers connect with the STUN server to figure out the IP address, port (allocated by NAT corresponding to the private IP address and port), and type of NAT the device is behind" / "the STUN server is always used as the first point of action. This is because using the TURN server to stream high-quality data is relatively expensive, adding an extra hop into the architecture" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/STUN | non-official | NAT会话穿越工具（STUN） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/STUN | non-official |
| 181 | Session Description Protocol (SDP) | SDP | "Once this information is obtained, it will be used as part of the Session Description Protocol (SDP)" / "SDP includes information like media type (audio or video), transport protocol (UDP, IP, etc.), media format, remote address, and port for the media" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Session_Description_Protocol | non-official | 会话描述协议（SDP） | https://rfc2cn.com/rfc4566.html | non-official |
| 182 | Traversal Using Relays around NAT (TURN) servers | TURN, TURN server | "In some scenarios, direct peer-to-peer connection may not be feasible because of restrictive firewalls and the STUN server fails to do its job. In these scenarios, we can use Traversal Using Relays around NAT (TURN) servers" / "A TURN server acts as an intermediary between the peers to communicate, where essentially one device sends a message to the TURN server, and then the TURN server relays this to another device" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traversal_Using_Relays_around_NAT | non-official | 使用中继穿透NAT（TURN） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/TURN | non-official |

# Glossary: ch08 — Architectural Designs and Patterns

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch08.html`.

> **Note on ch08 terms:** This chapter covers architectural design patterns (CDC, pub/sub, choreography/orchestration, big data architectures, EDA, cloud patterns) and open source distributed systems (HDFS, Apache Kafka). Most architectural patterns are general computing concepts with no single authoritative Chinese source; translations will be sourced from AWS Chinese documentation, Wikipedia zh-cn, or established Chinese technical references. AWS-managed service names (EMR, Redshift, Athena, Glue, Step Functions, Kinesis, MSK, ECS, DMS) are kept in English per AWS naming convention and marked "official" where confirmed in the AWS glossary. Apache ecosystem tools (Kafka, Spark, Hive, etc.) are kept in their English names as no standard Chinese translation exists.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 183 | change data capture (CDC) | CDC | "CDC is a sophisticated approach to data integration that tracks and captures changes in data at the source database in real time. This method pushes changes to various destinations, including other databases, data warehouses, or even real-time processing engines" / "CDC enables almost instantaneous data updates, allowing businesses to react to changes swiftly, which is crucial for dynamic pricing, stock updates, or personalized customer interactions" | https://aws.amazon.com/what-is/change-data-capture/ | non-official | 变更数据捕获（CDC） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/blogs/china/real-time-writing-of-cdc-data-into-redshift-data-warehouse-solution-in-multi-database-and-multi-table-scenarios/ | AWS official |
| 184 | audit-column-based CDC | timestamp column CDC, created_at/updated_at columns | "Time-based audit columns are a simple yet powerful method of implementing CDC. Adding timestamp columns such as created_at and updated_at to database tables makes it possible to track when each record was inserted or last modified" / "This method is particularly effective for applications that require a historical audit trail of changes. Its primary advantage lies in its simplicity and low overhead since it leverages the inherent capabilities of most RDBMSs" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Change_data_capture | non-official | 基于审计列的 CDC（时间戳列法） | https://blog.csdn.net/qq30211478/article/details/100690948 | non-official |
| 185 | log-based CDC | transaction log CDC | "Log-based CDC operates by reading transaction logs maintained by the database. Since these logs are designed to keep a record of all changes for data recovery and integrity, they serve as a reliable source for capturing data changes without additional overhead on the database itself" / "This method supports near real-time data integration, making it suitable for high-availability systems and those requiring immediate consistency across platforms" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Change_data_capture | non-official | 基于日志的 CDC | https://blog.csdn.net/xuejianxinokok/article/details/120330431 | non-official |
| 186 | stream-based CDC | DynamoDB Streams, log data streams | "An extension of log-based CDC, the stream-based CDC method formats log data into more consumable streams, making it easier for applications to process changes in real time" / "One such example is Amazon DynamoDB streams for publishing data updates in DynamoDB tables to different sources" | https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/database/exploring-change-data-capture-with-amazon-dynamodb/ | non-official | 基于流的 CDC（流式 CDC） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/Introduction.html | AWS official |
| 187 | table deltas | table snapshots, snapshot comparison | "Table deltas involve capturing changes based on the comparison of table snapshots at different times. This approach is straightforward: by periodically taking full snapshots of a table and comparing these snapshots, we can derive the changes" / "This method might introduce delays and is more resource intensive; thus, it is generally suited for systems with lower transaction volumes or where real-time sync is not critical" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Change_data_capture | non-official | 表快照比对法（增量快照） | https://blog.csdn.net/qq30211478/article/details/100690948 | non-official |
| 188 | trigger-based CDC | database triggers, DML triggers | "Trigger-based CDC uses database triggers to capture changes by automatically executing predefined procedures or SQL commands in response to events (INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE) occurring in a table" / "While this method is highly flexible and occurs in real time, managing database triggers can increase the load and complexity, especially in large-scale systems" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Change_data_capture | non-official | 基于触发器的 CDC | https://blog.csdn.net/qq30211478/article/details/100690948 | non-official |
| 189 | publisher-subscriber architecture | pub/sub architecture, pub/sub model, pub/sub | "Pub/sub architecture is a foundational model in event-driven programming that facilitates asynchronous communication among different parts of the system. This pattern is vital for applications where components need to interact efficiently without directly being linked to one another, thereby promoting loose coupling and scalable design" / "The pub/sub model abstracts the publisher from the subscribers through a broker or event bus, which handles the transmission of messages or events" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Publish%E2%80%93subscribe_pattern | non-official | 发布-订阅架构（发布/订阅模式） | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/publish-subscribe.html | AWS official |
| 190 | message broker | brokers, event bus | "Message brokers are key components in pub/sub architectures that manage the transmission of messages between producers (publishers) and consumers (subscribers). They ensure that messages are appropriately routed, maintained, and delivered, even in complex distributed systems" / "A typical message broker architecture involves queues that temporarily store messages and topics that categorize these messages. Publishers send messages to specific topics, while subscribers register their interest in one or more topics" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Message_broker | non-official | 消息代理 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/amazon-mq/latest/developer-guide/welcome.html | AWS official |
| 191 | message queue | queues, message queuing | "Message queues are a fundamental part of the messaging infrastructure that act as a temporary holding pen for messages waiting to be routed from publishers to subscribers" / "Apache Kafka is a prominent example of a message queue system. It is designed to handle high volumes of data while supporting high-throughput applications" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Message_queue | non-official | 消息队列 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/AWSSimpleQueueService/latest/SQSDeveloperGuide/welcome.html | AWS official |
| 192 | Advanced Message Queuing Protocol (AMQP) | AMQP | "Advanced Message Queuing Protocol (AMQP) is an open standard for passing messages between applications or organizations with a focus on high interoperability and reliability" / "AMQP mandates the behavior of the message provider and broker to ensure consistent message delivery, thus making it a favored choice for systems requiring robustness and functional predictability" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advanced_Message_Queuing_Protocol | non-official | 高级消息队列协议（AMQP） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E9%AB%98%E7%BA%A7%E6%B6%88%E6%81%AF%E9%98%9F%E5%88%97%E5%8D%8F%E8%AE%AE | non-official |
| 193 | choreography | decentralized workflow management, choreographed asynchronous events | "Choreography employs a decentralized approach to workflow management. In this model, each service knows what to do and when to do it based on the events it consumes. There is no central coordinator; instead, services perform their duties independently following the event notifications they receive" / "This method promotes loose coupling as services are designed to react to events rather than direct commands from a central authority. It enhances system scalability and resilience because services operate independently" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_choreography | non-official | 编配（去中心化服务协同） | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/saga-choreography.html | AWS official |
| 194 | orchestration | centralized workflow management, orchestrator | "Orchestration relies on a central coordinator, or an orchestrator, which controls the interaction between services by making decisions about the process flow, directing different services when to act" / "This approach simplifies monitoring and managing business logic, which can be advantageous for complex business processes that require precise coordination and strict compliance to workflows" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orchestration_(computing) | non-official | 编排（集中式服务协同） | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/saga-orchestration.html | AWS official |
| 195 | lambda architecture | batch layer, speed layer, serving layer | "Lambda architecture is a hybrid data-processing model that combines both batch and real-time processing methods. This architecture is designed to balance latency, throughput, and fault tolerance by processing and presenting data in three layers" / "Lambda architecture is beneficial for scenarios where it is crucial to have a near real-time view of data but also to handle massive datasets that are not feasible to process quickly" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lambda_architecture | non-official | Lambda 架构 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/blogs/china/build-a-big-data-lambda-architecture-for-batch-and-real-time-analytics-using-amazon-redshift/ | AWS official |
| 196 | kappa architecture | stream-processing layer, single-pipeline architecture | "An evolution of lambda architecture, kappa architecture simplifies operational complexity by treating all incoming data as a stream. This model proposes using a single processing layer (the stream-processing layer) for both real-time and historical data" / "By treating all data as a stream, kappa architecture removes the need to maintain two separate codebases and systems for batch and real-time processing, simplifying development and maintenance" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kappa_architecture | non-official | Kappa 架构 | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/539247209 | non-official |
| 197 | data lake architecture | centralized data repository, raw data storage | "Data lakes are centralized repositories designed to store, process, and secure large volumes of structured and unstructured data. Unlike traditional data warehouses, which generally store data in a structured, processed format, data lakes retain vast amounts of raw data in its native format" / "Data lake architecture not only supports big data storage but also integrates with various big data processing frameworks to enable comprehensive analytics and machine learning directly on the stored data" | https://aws.amazon.com/what-is/data-lake/ | non-official | 数据湖架构 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/blogs/china/build-a-real-time-data-lake-on-amazon-emr/ | AWS official |
| 198 | monolithic architecture | monolith, single executable | "In a monolithic architecture, all components of the software application are tightly integrated and run as a single service. This means that any changes made anywhere in the application can affect the entire system, which simplifies development and deployment processes in the early stages of a product's lifecycle" / "Monoliths are straightforward to develop, test, deploy, and scale horizontally since they are a single executable or deployment unit" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monolithic_application | non-official | 单体架构 | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/strangler-fig.html | AWS official |
| 199 | N-tier architecture | three-tier architecture, presentation layer, business logic layer, data management layer | "N-tier architectures break an application down into logical layers, each with a specific responsibility. Typically, these include at least three layers: presentation (frontend), business logic (middle-tier), and data management (backend). This is referred to as three-tier architecture" / "This architecture enhances maintainability by separating concerns among components, making it easier to manage, update, and scale individual parts of the system without affecting others" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multitier_architecture | non-official | N 层架构（三层架构） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-cn/%E5%A4%9A%E5%B1%82%E6%9E%B6%E6%9E%84 | non-official |
| 200 | microservice architecture | microservices, loosely coupled services | "A microservice architecture takes the principles of N-tier systems further by decomposing them into smaller, loosely coupled services. Each microservice focuses on a single functionality and can be developed, deployed, and scaled independently" / "The microservice architecture offers several benefits and challenges. It gives teams autonomy to develop, test, deploy, and scale services independently, speeding up development cycles and improving fault isolation" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microservices | non-official | 微服务架构 | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/strangler-fig.html | AWS official |
| 201 | event-driven architecture (EDA) | EDA, event-driven systems, event-driven programming | "EDA is a paradigm that orchestrates behavior around the production, detection, and consumption of events. This model offers a dynamic framework for applications to react to various state changes across distributed systems without the tight coupling of components, promoting scalability and responsiveness" / "Event-driven architecture represents a paradigm shift from traditional request-driven models, providing a flexible, scalable way to handle asynchronous system behaviors and data flows" | https://aws.amazon.com/what-is/eda/ | non-official | 事件驱动型架构（EDA） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/event-driven-architecture/ | AWS official |
| 202 | state machine | event-driven state machine, state, transition | "A state machine is a mathematical abstraction used to design algorithms based on a behavior model. A state machine reads a set of inputs and changes to a different state based on those inputs" / "A state machine is event-driven if the transition from one state to another is triggered by an event or a message (not based on consuming or parsing characters or based on conditions)" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finite-state_machine | non-official | 状态机 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/step-functions/latest/dg/welcome.html | AWS official |
| 203 | event sourcing | event-sourced persistence, event streams, immutable event log | "Unlike state-oriented implementations, event sourcing persists each state change as an immutable event. These events are the system's source of truth, from which the current state can be reconstructed" / "This approach supports historical state reconstruction, auditing, and complex system behaviors like rollback and replay of events to handle errors or system failures" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Event-driven_architecture | non-official | 事件溯源 | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/modernization-data-persistence/cqrs-pattern.html | AWS official |
| 204 | event store | append-only persistence, event journal | "Event-driven systems often use an event store, an append-only persistence optimized for storing events in sequence, as a single source of truth about the operations on both the application state and the data" / "This store ensures that all events are captured immutably, providing a reliable foundation for triggering actions, building projections, or reconstructing past states" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Event_store | non-official | 事件存储 | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/transactional-outbox.html | AWS official |
| 205 | command query responsibility segregation (CQRS) | CQRS, read model, write model | "CQRS is an architectural pattern that separates the operations that read data (queries) from the operations that update data (commands) into different interfaces" / "This separation allows read and write operations to be handled differently, optimizing performance, scalability, and security. For example, read models can be scaled independently from write models, which is particularly beneficial in systems where the number of read operations vastly exceeds the number of write operations" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Command_Query_Responsibility_Segregation | non-official | 命令查询责任分离（CQRS） | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/modernization-data-persistence/cqrs-pattern.html | AWS official |
| 206 | saga | saga pattern, compensating action, distributed transaction | "Sagas manage transactions that span multiple services, where each transaction step is paired with a compensating action for rollback if needed. They are based on either choreography or orchestration" / "In choreography, each service involved in the saga performs its transaction and publishes events that trigger the next steps in other services. In orchestration, a central coordinator directs each step of the saga, managing the sequence and triggering compensating transactions if necessary" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long-running_transaction | non-official | Saga 模式 | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/modernization-data-persistence/saga-pattern.html | AWS official |
| 207 | circuit breaker pattern | circuit breaker | "The circuit breaker pattern prevents a cascade of failures in distributed systems. When a particular service or operation fails to a threshold level, the circuit breaker 'trips,' and further attempts to invoke the failing service are halted temporarily, thus allowing it to recover and maintain system stability" / "These patterns work together to prevent cascading failures in distributed systems, ensuring that failures in one part of the system do not lead to widespread outages" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_breaker_design_pattern | non-official | 断路器模式 | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/circuit-breaker.html | AWS official |
| 208 | retry with backoff | exponential backoff, transient fault handling | "Retry with backoff involves reattempting failed operations with increasing delays between attempts, increasing the interval (backoff) progressively with each retry until a maximum number of retries is reached" / "This pattern is particularly useful when handling transient faults by providing time for the issue to resolve before retrying" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exponential_backoff | non-official | 带退避的重试（指数退避） | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/general/latest/gr/api-retries.html | AWS official |
| 209 | rate limiter | rate limiting, request throttling | "The rate limiter pattern controls the number of requests to a service within a specified time frame, preventing the system from being overwhelmed by a high volume of traffic or sudden spikes in demand" / "Rate limiting can be applied at various levels: per user, per service, or globally across a system. This pattern ensures that services continue to operate smoothly even under heavy load, reducing the likelihood of degraded performance or system crashes due to resource exhaustion" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rate_limiting | non-official | 限流（速率限制） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/apigateway/latest/developerguide/api-gateway-request-throttling.html | AWS official |
| 210 | domain-driven design (DDD) | DDD, domain model, core domain | "DDD focuses on the core domain logic and its complexity, basing the design on the domain's behavior and logic. This pattern encourages the creation of a domain model that incorporates business rules and behaviors, leading to a more intuitive correlation between the software and the business requirements it aims to fulfill" / "These patterns focus on aligning software design with business objectives" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domain-driven_design | non-official | 领域驱动设计（DDD） | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/modernization-decomposing-monoliths/decompose-subdomain.html | AWS official |
| 211 | decompose by subdomains | subdomain decomposition | "Decompose by subdomains builds on DDD in a microservice architecture to divide a system into subdomains, each handling a specific business capability. Each microservice manages a subdomain, which improves maintainability and scalability by allowing the microservice to evolve independently based on its unique business requirements" / "These patterns are essential for building modular, scalable systems that can evolve as business needs change" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domain-driven_design | non-official | 按子域分解 | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/modernization-decomposing-monoliths/decompose-subdomain.html | AWS official |
| 212 | API gateway | API routing, centralized entry point | "An API gateway acts as a centralized entry point for all client requests, providing a unified interface to a set of backend services. It handles tasks such as request routing, composition, and protocol translation" / "The API gateway can invoke multiple microservices to fulfill a single client request and aggregate the responses into a cohesive result. This pattern is particularly useful in microservice architectures as it simplifies client interactions and enhances security by centralizing access control and monitoring" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/API_management | non-official | API 网关 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/apigateway/latest/developerguide/welcome.html | AWS official |
| 213 | service mesh | sidecar proxy, service-to-service communication | "Service mesh architecture is an infrastructure layer that manages service-to-service communication within a microservice architecture. It provides advanced routing, load balancing, service discovery, and security features like mutual Transport Layer Security (TLS) authentication and traffic encryption" / "It is implemented using sidecar proxies that intercept and manage network traffic between services, providing observability and resilience without requiring changes to the application code" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_mesh | non-official | 服务网格 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/service-mesh/ | AWS official |
| 214 | anticorruption layer (ACL) | ACL, domain model protection | "The ACL serves as a protective barrier that prevents potentially disruptive data or requests from entering a system segment, thereby preserving the integrity of an application's core business model. It acts as a translator between different subsystems or external applications, ensuring that data exchanges do not corrupt the domain model" / "Each of these patterns provides targeted solutions to common problems in developing and managing cloud native applications" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domain-driven_design | non-official | 防腐层（ACL） | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/anti-corruption-layer.html | AWS official |
| 215 | strangler fig pattern | legacy system migration, incremental replacement | "This pattern is used for gradually transforming legacy systems by replacing specific pieces of functionality with new applications and services. Over time, these new components 'strangle' the old system, eventually replacing it entirely" / "Each of these patterns provides targeted solutions to common problems in developing and managing cloud native applications, offering pathways to more resilient, flexible, and scalable architectures" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strangler_fig_pattern | non-official | 绞杀者模式 | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/strangler-fig.html | AWS official |
| 216 | transactional outbox | outbox pattern, outbox table | "The transactional outbox pattern is used to ensure reliable messaging between services. When changes are made to the database, records are also added to an 'outbox' table within the same transaction" / "A separate process then sends these messages to the message queue or other destinations, ensuring data consistency across service boundaries" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inbox_and_outbox_pattern | non-official | 事务性发件箱模式 | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/transactional-outbox.html | AWS official |
| 217 | sidecar pattern | sidecar container, sidecar process | "The sidecar pattern is commonly used in microservice architectures. It involves deploying an additional container or process alongside a service container, providing platform-connected services such as monitoring, logging, configuration, and networking capabilities" / "This pattern allows for isolating infrastructure concerns from the application, making it cleaner and more modular" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sidecar_pattern | non-official | 边车模式 | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/sidecar.html | AWS official |
| 218 | backend for frontend (BFF) | BFF, client-specific backend | "This pattern involves creating separate backend services tailored for different types of clients, such as desktop browsers and mobile devices. It allows developers to optimize these backend services for specific user interfaces, enhancing the user experience by reducing overfetching or underfetching of data" / "Each of these patterns provides targeted solutions to common problems in developing and managing cloud native applications" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Backend_for_Frontend | non-official | 前端专属后端（BFF） | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/cloud-design-patterns/backends-for-frontends.html | AWS official |
| 219 | cellular architecture | cell-based architecture, self-contained cells | "Cellular architecture involves designing systems as collections of cells, where each cell operates independently but can communicate with other cells. Each cell is self-contained with its own data store and logic, capable of scaling autonomously" / "This approach is highly resilient since failure in one cell does not affect the others, and it supports evolutionary architecture by allowing each cell to evolve independently" | https://aws.amazon.com/solutions/guidance/cell-based-architecture-on-aws/ | non-official | 单元式架构（Cell-based 架构） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/solutions/guidance/cell-based-architecture-on-aws/ | AWS official |
| 220 | Hadoop Distributed File System (HDFS) | HDFS, EMRFS | "HDFS is an open source filesystem designed to run on commodity hardware. | 221 | NameNode | primary NameNode, secondary NameNode | "The NameNode is the central component of HDFS, responsible for managing the metadata and namespace of the filesystem. It keeps track of file locations and manages access to files by coordinating the storage across DataNodes. The NameNode is critical for the operation of HDFS; if it fails, the filesystem becomes inaccessible" / "The secondary NameNode is not a backup for the NameNode. Instead, it works alongside the NameNode to manage the filesystem's metadata. | 255 | at-least-once delivery | at-least-once semantics | "Every message is delivered at least once. # Glossary: ch10 — AWS Storage Services

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch10.html`.

> **Note on ch10 terms:** This chapter covers AWS-specific cloud storage services (EBS, EFS, S3, FSx) and managed database services (RDS, Aurora, DynamoDB, DocumentDB, Neptune, ElastiCache, OpenSearch, Timestream, Keyspaces). AWS service names are marked "official" where confirmed in the AWS glossary page or official AWS service documentation. Third-party open source technologies (MongoDB, Apache Cassandra, Gremlin, openCypher, SPARQL) and general technical standards (SAML, PCI DSS, HIPAA, FedRAMP, SMB, NVMe) are marked "non-official". Translations will be filled in a later step.

# Glossary: ch12 — AWS Messaging, Orchestration, Monitoring, and Access Management Services

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch12.html`.

> **Note on ch12 terms:** This chapter covers AWS messaging services (SQS, SNS, Kinesis sub-services), workflow orchestration (Step Functions, MWAA), monitoring (CloudWatch, EventBridge), and access management (IAM concepts, Cognito concepts, AppSync). AWS service names are marked "official" where confirmed in the official AWS glossary or service documentation. General technical standards (OAuth 2.0, OpenID, JWT, OpenTelemetry), open source frameworks (Apache Flink, Apache Zeppelin, Apache Parquet), and general concepts (PII, CIAM, federation) are marked "non-official". Services already documented in earlier chapters (MSK, Kinesis broadly, Step Functions, Apache Airflow, IAM, Cognito, CloudWatch broadly, AppSync, GraphQL, TLS, SAML, ZooKeeper, KRaft, ETL, exactly-once/at-least-once/at-most-once delivery) are not duplicated here.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|

# Glossary: ch14 — Designing a URL Shortener Service

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch14.html`.

> **Note on ch14 terms:** This chapter covers system design concepts (functional/nonfunctional requirements, URL shortening algorithms, unique ID generation) and AWS deployment architecture (Lambda, ECS Fargate, EKS, App Runner, DynamoDB, ElastiCache, CloudFront, Route 53, VPC networking components). AWS service names already documented in earlier chapters (DynamoDB, ElastiCache, CloudFront, CloudWatch, Cognito, WAF, Shield, Certificate Manager, Amplify, ECS, VPC, Multi-AZ, GSI, DAX, TTL, CDN) are not duplicated here. New AWS services from ch11 (compute) and ch09 (networking) referenced in ch14 are added here.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 367 | URL shortener service | short URL, shortened URL, URL shortening | "This chapter explores the design of a URL shortener service and discusses how to deploy the system on the AWS cloud" / "LinkedIn automatically shortens any URL that is part of a post because this helps increase post readability and user interaction" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/URL_shortening | non-official | 短网址服务（URL 缩短服务） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/URL%E7%B8%AE%E7%9F%AD%E6%9C%8D%E5%8A%A1 | non-official |
| 368 | functional requirements | functional requirement | "Functional requirements are the functionalities or features that the system offers to the end users" / "The main expectations from a URL shortener service are simple: The system takes input as a long URL and return a shortened URL" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Functional_requirement | non-official | 功能性需求 | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%8A%9F%E8%83%BD%E6%80%A7%E9%9C%80%E6%B1%82 | official (other source) |
| 369 | nonfunctional requirements | NFRs, non-functional requirements | "NFRs determine the constraints the system operates on and don't directly affect the user feature-wise, but they are important for ensuring the quality of system operations" / "Here are a few examples: Security, High availability, Observability, Low latency, Data stores, Fault tolerance, Interoperability" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-functional_requirement | non-official | 非功能性需求（NFR） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E9%9D%9E%E5%8A%9F%E8%83%BD%E6%80%A7%E9%9C%80%E6%B1%82 | official (other source) |
| 370 | hashing | hash function, hash-generation | "Hashing seems to be a viable solution to solve this problem: using a hash function such as MD5, which takes a long URL as input and returns a hash of this long URL as output" / "The hash-generation approach also requires a data store lookup (or multiple lookups in the case of collisions), which adds extra latency to the operation" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hash_function | non-official | 哈希（散列） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E6%95%A3%E5%88%97%E5%87%BD%E6%95%B8 | non-official |
| 371 | MD5 | — | "using a hash function such as MD5, which takes a long URL as input and returns a hash of this long URL as output" / "The length of the generated hash can be greater than 7, so the system should trim the generated hash to a reduced length" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MD5 | non-official | MD5（消息摘要算法 5） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/MD5 | non-official |
| 372 | hash collision | collision, collisions | "This solution is easy to implement but it has one problem: collision. Two different long URLs could possibly generate the same hash" / "even if the hash is different, the truncated length could be the same. At large scale, this can become a frequent problem, making short URL generation a bottleneck" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hash_collision | non-official | 哈希碰撞 | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%93%88%E5%B8%8C%E7%A2%B0%E6%92%9E | non-official |
| 373 | base62 encoding | base62 | "The sequence of alphanumeric characters from 0 to 9, a to z, and A to Z when used for encoding is referred to as base62 encoding" / "Note that when using base62 encoding on the counter values (decimal numbers), the short URL will be unique but not always of length 7" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Base62 | non-official | Base62 编码 | https://blog.csdn.net/q727778/article/details/127340547 | non-official |
| 374 | unique ID generation | unique IDs, globally unique ID | "A key point to consider here is that the long URL and short URL don't have to be related—this would essentially be a unique ID generation system, which can map the generated ID to a long URL" / "Think of it as a server initializing a counter value with '1' and then incrementing it every time a new request arrives" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universally_unique_identifier | non-official | 唯一 ID 生成 | https://blog.csdn.net/qq_34337272/article/details/84995386 | non-official |
| 375 | key generation service | KGS, key generation | "A single component responsible for pregeneration of short URLs. An important decision to make here is whether we give this additional responsibility to our URL shortener service or host it as an independent service" / "We'll go with the idea of hosting a separate service with the responsibility for generating unique keys and passing those on to our URL shortener service when requested" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Key_management | non-official | 密钥生成服务（KGS） | https://blog.csdn.net/qq_34337272/article/details/84995386 | non-official |
| 376 | Snowflake | Snowflake ID | "In 2010, X proposed another approach for generating 64-bit unique IDs: Snowflake. Snowflake can generate 4,096 unique IDs per millisecond with a combination of timestamp, machine identifier number, and sequence number" / "X used Snowflake because of the requirement for sorting and ensuring ordering guarantees" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snowflake_ID | non-official | Snowflake（雪花算法） | https://blog.csdn.net/luo15242208310/article/details/124243882 | non-official |
| 377 | ticket servers | ticket server, Flickr ticket servers | "We can take inspiration from Flickr's architecture, which uses MySQL's auto-increment feature with a REPLACE INTO query to get a globally unique new ID on every new query" / "To increase system availability and avoid a single point of failure, two database servers are used, starting with an even and odd number and offset as 2 to avoid collision" | https://code.flickr.net/2010/02/08/ticket-servers-distributed-unique-primary-keys-on-the-cheap/ | non-official | Ticket 服务器（票据服务器） | https://blog.csdn.net/qq_34337272/article/details/84995386 | non-official |
| 378 | multitenant system | multi-tenant, multitenant | "For example, assume that Google Drive leverages Bitly services to shorten URLs, but Google wants to share custom Google short URLs with users rather than Bitly short URLs" / "Designing and building multitenant systems requires an extensive thought process on system expectations" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multitenancy | non-official | 多租户系统 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/multi-tenancy/ | AWS official |
| 379 | physical separation | — | "Physical separation means deploying multiple systems in parallel, each serving a specific domain" / "The important point to consider here is whether the system has virtual (or logical) separation or physical separation" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multitenancy | non-official | 物理隔离 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/multi-tenancy/ | non-official |
| 380 | virtual separation | logical separation | "virtual separation means the same system caters to all the clients, with internal code separation or configurations" / "The important point to consider here is whether the system has virtual (or logical) separation or physical separation" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multitenancy | non-official | 虚拟隔离（逻辑隔离） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/what-is/multi-tenancy/ | non-official |
| 381 | Day 0 architecture | Day Zero architecture | "the system should be designed based on the Make It Work, Make It Right, Make It Fast principle, and thinking of those potential 10 million users from the start is not worth it" / "We'll start with Day 0 architecture and use AWS services that are easy to deploy and require less expertise. | 382 | Day N architecture | Day N | "Day N Architecture. | 383 | Make It Work, Make It Right, Make It Fast | Make it Work, Make it Right, Make it Fast principle | "the system should be designed based on the Make It Work, Make It Right, Make It Fast principle, and thinking of those potential 10 million users from the start is not worth it" / "Day 0 and Day N architecture is a general paradigm followed in Part 3" | https://wiki.c2.com/?MakeItWorkMakeItRightMakeItFast | non-official | 先让它跑起来，再让它做对，最后让它快起来 | https://blog.csdn.net/u012099641/article/details/53352656 | non-official |

Autoscaling ensures that instances are automatically added or removed based on the traffic without manual intervention" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 自动扩缩组（ASG） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/autoscaling/ec2/userguide/auto-scaling-groups.html | AWS official |

| 409 | DynamoDB global tables | global tables | "The application can be launched in multiple AWS regions, and DynamoDB global tables can be used to ensure that data is replicated across the regions" / "A key consideration for global tables is that there is approximately one second of replication lag between two or more regions" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/GlobalTables.html | official | DynamoDB 全局表 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/globaltables_reqs_bestpractices.html | AWS official |

| 411 | DynamoDB provisioned capacity mode | provisioned mode, provisioned capacity | "migrate to the provisioned mode if the traffic is predictable" / "Preplanning your capacity is helpful in resolving throttling with provisioned mode" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/provisioned-capacity-mode.html | official | DynamoDB 预置容量模式 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/capacity-mode.html | AWS official |

# Glossary: ch16 — Designing a Social Network and Newsfeed System

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch16.html`.

> **Note on ch16 terms:** This chapter covers social networking system design, including newsfeed/timeline architecture (push, pull, hybrid), search service (Elasticsearch, inverted index), user graph service, authentication scaling (Cognito vs custom), and multiregion deployments. AWS services already documented in earlier chapters (DynamoDB, ElastiCache, Redis, CloudFront, SNS, CloudWatch, Redshift, OpenSearch, Neptune, DAX, global tables, Route 53, Lambda, RCU/WCU, Snowflake, JWT, Cognito, OAuth) are not duplicated here. New AWS services introduced in ch16: Amazon SES, Amazon QuickSight, Amazon Keyspaces.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 414 | newsfeed | news feed, user feed, feed | "Newsfeed refers to the stream of posts and updates published by other users that a user typically sees as soon as they log into a social media app" / "The newsfeed for each website can be derived based on different factors" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News_feed | non-official | 信息流（动态流） | https://www.zhihu.com/question/20246139 | non-official |
| 415 | user timeline | timeline, user timelines | "newsfeed (or user timeline—we'll use both these terms interchangeably) component of the system" / "The user timeline service can directly serve the results from the cache data store for active users" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline | non-official | 用户时间线 | https://blog.csdn.net/demotest1/article/details/130058769 | non-official |
| 416 | push mechanism | push-based, fan-out on write, write-time fan-out | "The user post service retrieves all followers of a user, builds their timelines, and stores them in a data store. As the user comes online, the user's timeline can be served directly from the data store" / "The push approach makes writes complex (O(n) operation where n is number of followers) in favor of optimizing reads" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Push_technology | non-official | 推送机制（写时扇出） | https://blog.csdn.net/demotest1/article/details/130058769 | non-official |
| 417 | pull mechanism | pull-based, fan-in on read, read-time pull | "The user timeline is built in real time. As the user comes online, the system checks for new posts by people followed by the user and delivers them to the user's timeline" / "The pull mechanism is preferred for celebrity users, and the posts are retrieved in real time while constructing the user timelines" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pull_technology | non-official | 拉取机制（读时拉取） | https://blog.csdn.net/demotest1/article/details/130058769 | non-official |
| 418 | fan-out | fanout, message fan-out, fan-out operation | "SQS is widely used with SNS to meet message broker requirements without actually maintaining any infrastructure, such as a support use case of messaging fan-out" / "The orchestration component builds the user's timeline in cache, and the same new post event can be consumed by the search service" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fan-out_(software) | non-official | 扇出 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/streams/latest/dev/enhanced-consumers.html | AWS official |
| 419 | inverted index | inverted indices, reverse index | "What generally comes to mind when we hear the phrase 'search use cases' is indexing: leveraging inverted index solutions like Elasticsearch to offer search functionality for types of keywords" / "The indexing strategy should be quite efficient to power the search functionality at scale" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inverted_index | non-official | 倒排索引 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/opensearch-service/latest/developerguide/indexing.html | AWS official |
| 420 | Elasticsearch | Elastic | "leveraging inverted index solutions like Elasticsearch to offer search functionality for types of keywords" / "X uses an in-house solution called EarlyBird (an optimized Lucene index) to support searching" | https://www.elastic.co/elasticsearch | non-official | Elasticsearch（弹性搜索引擎） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/opensearch-service/latest/developerguide/what-is.html | non-official |
| 421 | Apache Lucene | Lucene, Lucene index | "X uses an in-house solution called EarlyBird (an optimized Lucene index) to support searching" / "The search service aggregator gathers data from all the instances of EarlyBird matching to the search query" | https://lucene.apache.org | non-official | Apache Lucene | https://lucene.apache.org | non-official |
| 422 | hashtag | hashtags, hash tag, topic tags | "Our system will create a newsfeed for a user based on people and topics (or hashtags) that the user chooses to follow" / "Retrieve all posts associated with a specific keyword or hashtag" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashtag | non-official | 话题标签（#标签） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E4%BA%95%E5%8F%B7%E6%A0%87%E7%AD%BE | non-official |
| 423 | eventual consistency | eventual consistent, eventually consistent | "Eventual consistency is fine for reflecting posts in the newsfeed. There is acceptable lag between when posts are published by a user and when those posts are reflected in followers' timelines" / "These read queries can be eventually consistent to ensure optimal customer experience" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eventual_consistency | non-official | 最终一致性 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/HowItWorks.ReadConsistency.html | AWS official |
| 424 | causal consistency | causal ordering, read your write | "The comment should not disappear due to eventual consistency; this could create confusion about whether it was successfully posted" / "An important point to address is that when a user publishes a comment, they should see it with causal consistency" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Causal_consistency | non-official | 因果一致性 | https://blog.csdn.net/wufaliang003/article/details/78313011 | non-official |
| 425 | "read your write" consistency | read-your-writes, read your own writes | "To achieve 'read your write' consistency, the user should be tied to the leader node (or enable consistent reads for required operations) for the time being" / "A helpful idea in this situation is 'read your write' consistency" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eventual_consistency#Read-your-writes_consistency | non-official | 读己之写一致性 | https://blog.csdn.net/wufaliang003/article/details/78313011 | non-official |
| 426 | Change Data Capture | CDC, CDC events | "The orchestration component builds the user's timeline in cache, and the same new post event can be consumed by the search service to offer search functionality on tags or keywords" / "The post workflow orchestration is an async layer that comes into the picture once the post from a user is saved to a persistent database" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Change_data_capture | non-official | 变更数据捕获（CDC） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/Streams.html | AWS official |
| 427 | DynamoDB Streams | DynamoDB stream | "we can build the keyword search functionality on top of DynamoDB by streaming the user post data to an Amazon OpenSearch cluster via DynamoDB streams" / "streaming the user post data to an Amazon OpenSearch cluster via DynamoDB streams, as shown in Figure 16-5" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/Streams.html | official | DynamoDB 流 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/Streams.html | AWS official |
| 428 | Amazon Keyspaces | Keyspaces, Amazon Keyspaces for Apache Cassandra | "The data in DynamoDB should be stored in a way that makes the read queries efficient...a NoSQL database like Amazon DynamoDB or Amazon Keyspaces (compatible with Apache Cassandra)" / "DynamoDB offers automatic horizontal scaling and consistent single-digit millisecond latency at any scale" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/keyspaces/latest/devguide/what-is-keyspaces.html | official | Amazon Keyspaces（Apache Cassandra 兼容） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/keyspaces/ | AWS official |
| 429 | Amazon Simple Email Service | SES, Amazon SES | "We can use Amazon SNS and Amazon SES to deliver notifications to the users, and we can integrate monitoring tools like Amazon CloudWatch" / "SES to deliver notifications to the users" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | Amazon SES（云邮件发送服务） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/ses/ | AWS official |

| 435 | dual write strategy | dual write, double write | "During the migration window, the system should follow a dual write strategy. This means the system reads data from both the databases" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Replication_(computing) | non-official | 双写策略 | https://blog.csdn.net/qq_36279445/article/details/122183474 | non-official |

| 440 | PCI DSS | Payment Card Industry Data Security Standard, PCI | "Compliance regulations, such as HIPAA and PCI DSS" / "Cognito is compliant with these standards" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Payment_Card_Industry_Data_Security_Standard | non-official | 支付卡行业数据安全标准（PCI DSS） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/compliance/pci-dss-level-1-faqs/ | AWS official |

# Glossary: ch18 — Designing a Hotel Reservation System

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch18.html`.

> **Note on ch18 terms:** This chapter covers hotel/property reservation system design, including property onboarding (Step Functions state machine), property search (Elasticsearch/OpenSearch, Geohash, quadtree, GraphQL federation), property booking (pessimistic/optimistic locking, Redlock, idempotency, payment architecture), dynamic pricing (SageMaker Feature Store), and scaling with Aurora Limitless Database. AWS services already documented in earlier chapters (DynamoDB, Aurora, S3, Elasticsearch/OpenSearch, Redis, CloudFront, API Gateway, Cognito, Route 53, Lambda, SNS, SES, Step Functions, SQS, EKS, EC2, ELB, CDC/DynamoDB Streams, state machine, idempotence, back pressure, GraphQL federation, TTL, inverted index, exactly-once semantics) are not duplicated here. New services and concepts introduced in ch18 are listed below.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 447 | Aurora Limitless Database | Limitless Database, Aurora Limitless | "Aurora Limitless Database capability. This feature is helpful for managing millions of write transactions per second and petabytes of data with no sharding overhead" / "The user talks to a single writer endpoint, which internally communicates with multiple distributed transaction routers" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/AuroraUserGuide/limitless.html | official | Amazon Aurora Limitless 数据库 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/en_us/AmazonRDS/latest/AuroraUserGuide/limitless.html | AWS official |
| 448 | Amazon Location Service | Location Service | "the system can integrate with Amazon Location Service to offer capabilities like maps and points of interest" / "Amazon Location Service to offer capabilities like maps and points of interest" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/location/latest/developerguide/what-is.html | official | Amazon Location Service（亚马逊位置服务） | https://aws.amazon.com/location/ | AWS official |
| 449 | Amazon SageMaker Feature Store | SageMaker Feature Store, Feature Store | "Amazon SageMaker Feature Store is another service that can be used as a repository to store all the pricing features" / "The pricing system database is continuously updated in the background based on the ML inferences" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/sagemaker/latest/dg/feature-store.html | official | Amazon SageMaker 特征存储（Feature Store） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/blogs/china/new-store-discover-and-share-machine-learning/ | AWS official |
| 450 | Geohash | geohash, geohashing | "Geohash is helpful for encoding latitudes and longitudes into an alphanumeric string" / "Geohash is created by recursively dividing it into smaller grids" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geohash | non-official | 地理哈希（Geohash） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geohash | non-official |
| 451 | quadtree | quad tree, quadtree | "The most popular implementation is quadtree (Booking.com leverages quadtree to implement its points-of-interest feature), which works on a similar concept as Geohash" / "A particular grid is constantly divided into four subparts until the area has the desired number of places" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quadtree | non-official | 四叉树 | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E5%9B%9B%E5%8F%89%E6%A0%91 | non-official |
| 452 | pessimistic locking | pessimistic concurrency, pessimistic lock | "Pessimistic locking requires acquiring a lock at read, and the lock is only released after the operation is completed" / "acquiring the lock at the read step is called pessimistic locking" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Record_locking | non-official | 悲观锁 | https://javaguide.cn/java/concurrent/optimistic-lock-and-pessimistic-lock.html | non-official |
| 453 | optimistic locking | optimistic concurrency, optimistic lock | "Optimistic locking allows multiple user requests to read and update the data simultaneously" / "acquiring it at the update step is called optimistic locking" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Optimistic_concurrency_control | non-official | 乐观锁 | https://javaguide.cn/java/concurrent/optimistic-lock-and-pessimistic-lock.html | non-official |
| 454 | Redlock algorithm | Redlock, Redis distributed lock | "Redis recommends the Redlock algorithm for proper implementation in distributed environments" / "This implementation works well with a single Redis instance, but addition and deletion become tricky with Redis clusters" | https://redis.io/docs/manual/patterns/distributed-locks/ | non-official | Redlock 算法（分布式锁算法） | https://redis.io/docs/manual/patterns/distributed-locks/ | non-official |
| 455 | dynamic pricing | dynamic price, room pricing | "The booking platforms in general don't keep a static price. The price is adjusted based on multiple factors, such as location, date-range selection, the number of days of the stay, and the popularity of the property" / "architecture should give independence to the owners to determine a fixed price and manage it on their own or leave the decision to the booking system" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynamic_pricing | non-official | 动态定价 | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E5%8A%A8%E6%80%81%E5%AE%9A%E4%BB%B7 | non-official |
| 456 | webhook | webhooks, HTTP callback | "A webhook is a callback sent from a source system to a destination system to facilitate event-driven communication among multiple systems" / "the backend system posts an HTTP payload to the frontend system endpoint, and the same is reflected to the customer on the web UI" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Webhook | non-official | Webhook（回调钩子） | https://blog.csdn.net/qq_36279445/article/details/122183474 | non-official |
| 457 | points of interest | POI, points-of-interest feature | "Booking.com leverages quadtree to implement its points-of-interest feature" / "to look for points of interest near the booked property" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Point_of_interest | non-official | 兴趣点（POI） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%85%B4%E8%B6%A3%E7%82%B9 | non-official |
| 458 | two-phase commit | 2PC, two phase commit | "two-phase commit is used for multishard transactions" / "same shard query is committed via one-phase commit, and two-phase commit is used for multishard transactions" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-phase_commit_protocol | non-official | 两阶段提交（2PC） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E4%BA%8C%E9%98%B6%E6%AE%B5%E6%8F%90%E4%BA%A4 | non-official |
| 459 | one-phase commit | 1PC, single-phase commit | "The same shard query is committed via one-phase commit" / "one-phase commit, and two-phase commit is used for multishard transactions" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-phase_commit_protocol | non-official | 单阶段提交（1PC） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E4%BA%8C%E9%98%B6%E6%AE%B5%E6%8F%90%E4%BA%A4 | non-official |
| 460 | hash-based partitioning | hash partitioning, hash-based sharding | "The sharded tables are partitioned via hash-based partitioning by taking the hash of the shard key to 64 bits" / "the ranges of 64 bit space are assigned to the shards" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shard_(database_architecture) | non-official | 基于哈希的分区（哈希分片） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/HowItWorks.Partitions.html | non-official |
| 461 | bounded clocks | bounded clock, time bounds | "Aurora's custom implementation of bounded clocks" / "The EC2 time-sync service offers three entries of time: current approximate time, earliest possible time, and latest possible time" | https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/database/amazon-aurora-limitless-database-is-now-generally-available/ | non-official | 有界时钟 | https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/database/amazon-aurora-limitless-database-is-now-generally-available/ | non-official |
| 462 | pay-in system | pay-in, PayIn service | "The payment system's core responsibility is to allow customers to pay via their preferred payment option and record the payment (pay-in system)" / "Responsible for customer payments: from customer to booking system or from booking system to customer (in the case of refunds)" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Payment_system | non-official | 收款系统（Pay-in 服务） | https://blog.csdn.net/weixin_43860033/article/details/120432316 | non-official |
| 463 | pay-out system | pay-out, PayOut service | "making those payments to the property owners (pay-out system) after taking out the booking commission" / "Handles the logic of settling the payments with property owners" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Payment_system | non-official | 付款系统（Pay-out 服务） | https://blog.csdn.net/weixin_43860033/article/details/120432316 | non-official |
| 464 | reconciliation system | payment reconciliation, reconciliation | "For any payment system, there is a requirement of an additional reconciliation system, ensuring that the debits and credits for all payments are balanced, no discrepancy is created, and transaction integrity is maintained" / "The reconciliation system operates based on inputs from internal and external systems" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconciliation_(accounting) | non-official | 对账系统（支付对账） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/solutions/financial-services/ | non-official |

# Glossary: ch20 — Designing a Video-Processing Pipeline for a Streaming Service

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch20.html`.

> **Note on ch20 terms:** This chapter covers the design of a video-processing pipeline for streaming services (Netflix, Amazon Prime Video, YouTube), including video encoding (codecs, bitrate ladder, VMAF quality scoring), content protection (DRM, Widevine, PlayReady), adaptive streaming protocols (HLS, MPEG-DASH), content distribution (Open Connect Appliances, CDN, Origin Shield), and AWS Elemental media services (MediaConvert, MediaLive, MediaPackage, MediaStore). AWS services already documented in earlier chapters (S3, DynamoDB, CloudFront, Step Functions, EventBridge, X-Ray, EKS, CloudWatch, Cassandra/Keyspaces, Elasticsearch, GraphQL federation, microservices, CDN, autoscaling) are not duplicated here. New AWS services introduced in ch20: AWS Elemental MediaConvert, MediaLive, MediaPackage, MediaStore, and AWS Direct Connect. CloudFront advanced features (Origin Shield, regional edge caches, PoPs) are also newly introduced.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 465 | video on demand | VoD, video-on-demand | "We'll focus the discussion on VoD as the main feature of the video-processing pipeline and spend some time on live streaming in the AWS Day 0 architecture discussion" / "In this chapter, we'll focus on building a video-processing pipeline for streaming services like Netflix, Amazon Prime Video, and YouTube" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Video_on_demand | non-official | 视频点播（VoD） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%A7%86%E9%A2%91%E7%82%B9%E6%92%AD | non-official |

| 469 | bitrate | bit rate, bitrates | "Bitrate means the number of bits used per second, which helps determine the speed at which chunks can be served on a user's device from system servers" / "High quality (high bitrate): 4K, 1080p, 720p" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bit_rate | non-official | 码率（比特率） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/704361562 | non-official |

| 471 | shot-based encoding | shot-based encode | "Shot-based encoding improves the overall compression efficiency and is a successor to title-based encoding" / "Netflix systems employ multiple mechanisms, such as title-based encoding and shot-based encoding, to ensure that maximum-quality videos are served to users at a lower bandwidth usage" | https://netflixtechblog.com | non-official | 镜头级编码 | https://blog.csdn.net/sinat_33231573/article/details/108486240 | non-official |

| 483 | CloudFront Origin Shield | Origin Shield | "Origin Shield is an additional layer (or shield) in the front of the origin storage, which is helpful for improving the cache hit ratio and reducing the load on the origin" / "Once the origin shield is introduced, it acts as the caching layer between the origin and other RECs so that if the data is not cached at an REC, it is retrieved from the origin shield" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/origin-shield.html | official | CloudFront 源护盾（Origin Shield） | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/origin-shield.html | AWS official |
| 484 | regional edge caches | RECs, regional edge cache | "The first points of contact for users via CloudFront are the edge locations, or PoPs. If data is not cached at the edge locations, the request tries to retrieve data from regional caches—also called regional edge caches (RECs)—and finally from the origin if data is missing at the RECs as well" / "once the origin shield is introduced, it acts as the caching layer between the origin and other RECs" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/HowCloudFrontWorks.html | official | 区域边缘缓存（REC） | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/origin-shield.html | AWS official |
| 485 | points of presence | PoPs, edge locations, embedded PoPs | "The first points of contact for users via CloudFront are the edge locations, or PoPs" / "CloudFront also launched embedded PoPs to directly cache the content at the ISPs' facilities similar to Netflix-managed OCAs" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/Introduction.html | official | 边缘站点（接入点，PoP） | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/origin-shield.html | AWS official |
| 486 | AWS Elemental MediaConvert | Elemental MediaConvert, MediaConvert | "the most important service for building a video-processing pipeline is AWS Elemental MediaConvert. This service converts source video into multiple formats and makes them available for streaming on different kinds of devices—a core video-encoding task" / "Elemental MediaConvert takes input as source video, encodes the video into different formats, and stores the encoded videos to S3" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/mediaconvert/latest/ug/what-is.html | official | AWS Elemental MediaConvert（文件视频处理服务） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/mediaconvert/ | AWS official |
| 487 | AWS Elemental MediaLive | Elemental MediaLive, MediaLive | "video content produced from high-quality cameras consumes a lot of storage space...Elemental MediaLive plays a critical role in achieving low latency and offers video encoding and compression at the source" / "The high-quality video content is encoded at the source in real time and then uploaded to the cloud for further processing and distribution" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/medialive/latest/ug/what-is.html | official | AWS Elemental MediaLive（直播视频编码服务） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/medialive/ | AWS official |
| 488 | AWS Elemental MediaPackage | Elemental MediaPackage, MediaPackage | "Once the video stream reaches the AWS cloud, it is consumed by Elemental MediaPackage for content protection and delivery. MediaPackage helps with manifest creation of the video, content protection via DRM such as Google Widevine and Microsoft PlayReady" / "We discussed DRM protection...AWS Elemental MediaPackage services can help with protecting content and distributing it to CloudFront" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/mediapackage/latest/ug/what-is.html | official | AWS Elemental MediaPackage（视频打包与内容保护服务） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/mediapackage/ | AWS official |
| 489 | AWS Elemental MediaStore | Elemental MediaStore, MediaStore | "AWS offers the Elemental MediaStore service, which is specifically designed and optimized to store media content and serve the live content with low latency" / "In Figure 20-5, we used S3 as a video storage solution. AWS offers the Elemental MediaStore service" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/mediastore/latest/ug/what-is.html | official | AWS Elemental MediaStore（媒体优化存储服务） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/mediastore/ | AWS official |
| 490 | AWS Direct Connect | Direct Connect | "The next technology used for faster uploads is AWS Direct Connect, which ensures that the video streams are uploaded via the AWS global network and don't face the congestion challenges of the public internet" / "AWS Direct Connect, which ensures that the video streams are uploaded via the AWS global network" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/directconnect/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html | official | Amazon Direct Connect（亚马逊专用网络连接） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/directconnect/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html | AWS official |
| 491 | ingestion system | video ingestion pipeline, ingestion | "the first step in designing our video-processing pipeline (which we'll call our ingestion system hereafter) is uploading videos created by production houses" / "A video uploader is a platform for production houses to upload their content and then track progress to know when the content is actually available for viewing" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_ingestion | non-official | 摄取系统（数据接入管道） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/opensearch-service/latest/developerguide/what-is.html | non-official |
| 492 | network congestion | congestion avoidance, network congestion scenarios | "Streaming video content from an edge location to the user's location consumes a lot of internet bandwidth, and if the number of users is too large, the high traffic can cause network congestion" / "Network congestion can lead to degradation in video quality" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Network_congestion | non-official | 网络拥塞（拥塞规避） | https://blog.csdn.net/xiaofei0859/article/details/51057809 | non-official |
| 493 | fail-fast | fail fast, failing fast | "The whole point of validation at every step of the pipeline is failing fast or detecting issues at the earliest possible moment" / "The most important takeaway from our review of the overall architecture, which should be applied as a general principle for architecting any new system, is fail-fast behavior" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fail-fast | non-official | 快速失败（fail-fast 原则） | https://blog.csdn.net/qq_36652619/article/details/107895688 | non-official |
| 494 | distributed tracing | request tracing, request ID | "We can use tools like AWS X-Ray, Zipkin, and OpenTracing to trace the application requests in a distributed system architecture" / "The key design principle in implementing distributed tracing is using the same request ID (a unique identifier to process a request received by service) across the applications" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tracing_(software) | non-official | 分布式追踪 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/en_us/xray/latest/devguide/xray-services.html | AWS official |
