# Glossary: ch05 — Load Balancing Approaches and Techniques

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch05.html`.

> **Note on ch05 terms:** This chapter covers load balancing concepts, algorithms, and deployment strategies. AWS service names (Application Load Balancer, Network Load Balancer, Amazon Route 53) use their official Chinese-document names where available. General networking terms (reverse proxy, forward proxy, NAT) use widely accepted Chinese translations from official AWS documentation or authoritative Chinese technical sources. Algorithm names (round robin, least connections) are confirmed from AWS ELB Chinese documentation. Terms like Nginx and HAProxy have no standard Chinese name and are kept in English.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 495 | load balancer | LB, LBs, load balancers | "An LB is a crucial component in network architecture that distributes incoming network traffic across multiple servers to enhance the performance and accessibility of applications, websites, or databases" / "When a server is unresponsive, is incapable of handling new requests, or exhibits an increased error rate, the LB halts traffic to that server and routes it to another available server" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 负载均衡器 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/elasticloadbalancing/latest/userguide/what-is-load-balancing.html | AWS official |
| 496 | server pool | server farm, backend servers | "load balancing comes into play to efficiently distribute incoming network traffic across the group of backend servers, often referred to as a server pool" / "The even load distribution can be dependent on the chosen algorithm, but the idea behind an LB is balancing load among all the servers" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Server_farm | non-official | 服务器池 | https://blog.csdn.net/weixin_44259720/article/details/122995882 | non-official |
| 497 | Elastic Load Balancers | ELBs, Elastic Load Balancing | "AWS offers Elastic Load Balancers (ELBs) in different types as application, network, and gateway LBs" / "Some examples of network LBs are AWS Network Load Balancer (NLB), Azure Load Balancer" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 弹性负载均衡（ELB） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/elasticloadbalancing/latest/userguide/what-is-load-balancing.html | AWS official |
| 498 | reverse proxy | reverse proxies | "A reverse proxy sits as a gateway between the internet and web servers, intercepting incoming requests from clients and routing them to the appropriate backend servers" / "Reverse proxies are commonly used to improve security by hiding backend server details from clients" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reverse_proxy | non-official | 反向代理 | https://blog.csdn.net/bigtree_3721/article/details/78007791 | non-official |
| 499 | forward proxy | forward proxies | "a forward proxy acts as an intermediary between client devices and the internet, mediating the traffic in between" / "Forward proxies are commonly used to enforce corporate access control policies, filter web content, improve privacy and security by masking client IP addresses" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proxy_server | non-official | 正向代理 | https://blog.csdn.net/bigtree_3721/article/details/78007791 | non-official |
| 500 | API gateway | API gateways | "An API gateway is a specialized type of reverse proxy acting as a facade that provides a single unified entry point for client applications to access multiple backend services or APIs" / "API gateways streamline API management by enforcing security policies, rate limiting, authentication, authorization, request and response transformations" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/API_management | non-official | API 网关 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/apigateway/latest/developerguide/welcome.html | AWS official |
| 501 | Amazon API Gateway | API Gateway (AWS) | "AWS offers Amazon API Gateway as a fully managed service that makes it easy for developers to create, publish, maintain, monitor, and secure APIs at any scale" / "API gateways are commonly used in microservice architectures to abstract complexities and provide a unified interface" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | Amazon API Gateway | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/apigateway/latest/developerguide/welcome.html | AWS official |
| 502 | Secure Sockets Layer | SSL, SSL termination | "Reverse proxies are commonly used to improve security by hiding backend server details from clients. They also improve scalability and performance by offloading tasks like Secure Sockets Layer (SSL) termination" / "one example is SSL/TLS offloading at the load balancer and decrypted traffic being forwarded to the backend servers over HTTP" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_Layer_Security | non-official | 安全套接字层（SSL） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/acm/latest/userguide/acm-overview.html | AWS official |
| 503 | global server load balancing | GSLB | "Global server load balancing (GSLB) operates at a larger scale, typically by distributing traffic across different data centers or geographical regions" / "This strategy is fundamental for serving content to users worldwide, improving performance, and ensuring compliance with regional regulations" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 全局服务器负载均衡（GSLB） | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 504 | application delivery controllers | ADCs, application delivery controller | "Application delivery controllers (ADCs) are computer network devices in a data center, often part of an application delivery network (ADN) that manages traffic flow to the servers" / "Some ADCs have a real-time view of the servers and forward requests based on the health and capacity of the data center" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Application_delivery_controller | non-official | 应用交付控制器（ADC） | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 505 | application delivery network | ADN | "Application delivery controllers (ADCs) are computer network devices in a data center, often part of an application delivery network (ADN) that manages traffic flow to the servers" / "ADN that manages traffic flow to the servers" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Application_delivery_network | non-official | 应用交付网络（ADN） | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 506 | local load balancing | local LB | "local load balancing manages traffic within a specific data center or a localized network, functioning akin to reverse proxies" / "It balances the load among servers within the same geographical region or network segment" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 本地负载均衡 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 507 | virtual IP address | VIP, virtual IP | "Clients' requests seamlessly connect to the LB, which operates using a virtual IP (VIP) address" / "A VIP is an address not directly tied to a physical machine; instead, multiple machines share the same VIP" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virtual_IP_address | non-official | 虚拟IP地址（VIP） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/129026081 | non-official |
| 508 | three-tier architecture | three-tier, 3-tier architecture | "In a three-tier architecture, LBs can be placed between the server instances of the three tiers" / "LBs placed between clients and web servers serve as the entry point for incoming user requests" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multitier_architecture | non-official | 三层架构 | https://blog.csdn.net/qq_39909999/article/details/109255655 | non-official |
| 509 | round robin | round-robin algorithm | "This algorithm distributes incoming requests equally among servers in a sequential manner. Each server receives a request in turn, ensuring a balanced workload distribution" / "Nginx distributes the requests across the backend servers using its default round-robin load balancing algorithm" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Round-robin_scheduling | non-official | 轮询 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/elasticloadbalancing/latest/userguide/how-elastic-load-balancing-works.html | AWS official |
| 510 | weighted round robin | weighted round-robin algorithm | "this algorithm assigns different weights to servers based on their capabilities or capacities. The traffic is proportionately divided between the servers based on the weights" / "making it beneficial for balancing workloads on servers with varying capacities" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weighted_round_robin | non-official | 加权轮询 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 511 | hash-based algorithms | hash-based load balancing, IP hash | "These algorithms use specific attributes from the request (like the source IP or content) to generate a hash key, which is then mapped to a server" / "This ensures that requests with similar attributes are consistently directed to the same server, aiding in session persistence" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hash_function | non-official | 基于哈希的算法 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 512 | static load balancing algorithms | static algorithms, static load balancing | "Static algorithms make load assignments based on existing knowledge of server configurations, disregarding changes in server states at runtime" / "They are less complex and are typically implemented in a single router or commodity machine where all requests converge" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 静态负载均衡算法 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 513 | dynamic load balancing algorithms | dynamic algorithms, dynamic load balancing | "Dynamic algorithms make load assignments based on current or recent server states. They engage in communication with servers, incurring communication overhead" / "Maintaining state information significantly complicates the algorithm's design, resulting in better request-forwarding decisions" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 动态负载均衡算法 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 514 | least connections | least connections algorithm | "Servers with the fewest active connections receive new requests. This algorithm aims to distribute traffic to servers that are less busy, effectively managing the current server load" / "it may not consider server capacity, which can lead to potential load imbalances" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 最少连接 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/elasticloadbalancing/latest/userguide/how-elastic-load-balancing-works.html | AWS official |
| 515 | least response time | least response time algorithm | "This algorithm directs requests to the server with the quickest response time. It monitors servers' successful response times and channels traffic to the most responsive server" / "ensuring efficient resource utilization" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 最短响应时间 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 516 | least loaded | least loaded algorithm | "This algorithm assesses the current load on each server and directs new requests to the one with the least load" / "This dynamic approach ensures efficient resource distribution based on the current server's performance parameters, such as CPU and memory utilization" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 最低负载 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 517 | session persistence | session persistence in load balancers | "Static and dynamic algorithms must prioritize the health of hosting servers while maintaining a state to store session information for various clients" / "Sticky sessions achieve this by 'sticking' a user's session to a specific server for the duration of their session" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 会话持久性（会话保持） | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 518 | sticky sessions | session affinity, session persistence | "Sticky sessions, also known as session persistence or session affinity, are a mechanism used in load balancing to ensure that a user's requests are consistently directed to the same server within a server farm" / "Sticky sessions achieve this by 'sticking' a user's session to a specific server for the duration of their session" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 粘性会话（会话黏着） | https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/658635474 | non-official |
| 519 | cookie-based session affinity | cookie-based affinity, session cookies | "The LB uses a session cookie generated during the initial connection to identify and route subsequent requests from the same user to the server indicated in the cookie" / "L7 LBs can achieve session persistence by using cookies to keep track of client sessions" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HTTP_cookie | non-official | 基于Cookie的会话亲和性 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 520 | source IP affinity | IP affinity, client IP affinity | "Requests from a particular IP address are consistently directed to the same server. This method can be less effective in scenarios where users are behind network address translation (NAT) or use proxy servers" / "This ensures that subsequent requests from the same client are directed to the same backend server" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 源IP亲和性 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 521 | stateful load balancers | stateful LBs, stateful load balancing | "Stateful LBs maintain session information, keeping track of the state of each client-server interaction. They store data related to the ongoing sessions, such as session cookies or other session identifiers" / "This enables the LB to consistently direct subsequent requests from the same client to the server that originally handled the initial request" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 有状态负载均衡器 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 522 | stateless load balancers | stateless LBs, stateless load balancing | "Stateless LBs do not retain session information or state data. Each request is treated independently, and there's no knowledge or tracking of previous interactions between clients and servers" / "Requests are distributed purely based on the load balancing algorithm without any consideration for session continuity" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 无状态负载均衡器 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 523 | network address translation | NAT, DNAT, destination NAT | "L4 LBs are often implemented as NATs, which distribute traffic among backend servers" / "Clients connect to the service VIP and the LB forwards packets to backend servers by changing the destination IP address (or DNAT)" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Network_address_translation | non-official | 网络地址转换（NAT） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/vpc/latest/userguide/vpc-nat-gateway.html | AWS official |
| 524 | DNS load balancers | DNS LBs, DNS-based load balancing | "DNS LBs distribute traffic at the DNS level by resolving domain names to different IP addresses based on predefined policies, serving as tier-0 LBs" / "They are easy to set up and can provide basic load balancing functionality but may lack some advanced features compared to L4 and L7 LBs" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Round-robin_DNS | non-official | DNS负载均衡器 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/Route53/latest/DeveloperGuide/routing-policy.html | AWS official |
| 525 | ECMP routers | equal-cost multipath routers, ECMP | "ECMP routers are a method of load balancing typically used at the network layer as a tier-1 LB. They distribute traffic across multiple paths or links with equal cost" / "ECMP routers operate by hashing packet headers to determine the appropriate path for each packet" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equal-cost_multi-path_routing | non-official | 等价多路径路由器（ECMP） | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 526 | network load balancers | NLBs, network LBs, L4 LBs | "Operating at the transport layer (L4) of the OSI model, network LBs handle traffic based on network variables like IP addresses and destination ports" / "L4 LBs are often implemented as NATs, which distribute traffic among backend servers" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 网络负载均衡器（NLB） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/elasticloadbalancing/latest/network/introduction.html | AWS official |
| 527 | application load balancers | ALBs, application LBs, L7 LBs | "Application LBs operate at the application layer (L7) of the OSI model, enabling them to make routing decisions based on application-specific parameters" / "Application LBs are well suited for complex applications and environments requiring advanced routing capabilities" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 应用程序负载均衡器（ALB） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/elasticloadbalancing/latest/application/introduction.html | AWS official |
| 528 | direct server return | DSR mode, DSR | "In this mode, the TCP connection is established directly between the client and the backend server. The LB changes only the destination MAC address of the packets, allowing backend servers to respond directly to clients" / "DSR mode can be useful for scenarios like WebSocket connections and large reply traffic" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Direct_Server_Return | non-official | 直接服务器返回（DSR）模式 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 529 | hardware load balancers | hardware LBs | "Hardware LBs are physical devices dedicated to load balancing tasks. These appliances require proprietary hardware and are typically deployed in data centers or on-premises environments" / "While hardware LBs offer high performance and reliability, they can be expensive to procure and maintain" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 硬件负载均衡器 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 530 | software load balancers | software LBs | "Software LBs are implemented as software applications running on standard server hardware or virtual machines" / "Software LBs are cost-effective alternatives to hardware solutions and are well suited for dynamic or cloud-based infrastructures" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 软件负载均衡器 | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 531 | load balancer as a service | LBaaS | "In the realm of cloud computing, load balancer as a service (LBaaS) has emerged, where cloud providers offer load balancing services" / "The key advantages of LBaaSs are ease of use, flexibility, metered costs, and robust auditing and monitoring services" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Load_balancing_(computing) | non-official | 负载均衡即服务（LBaaS） | https://blog.csdn.net/FrancisHe/article/details/72871549 | non-official |
| 532 | SSL/TLS offloading | SSL offloading, TLS termination, SSL termination | "one example is SSL/TLS offloading at the load balancer and decrypted traffic being forwarded to the backend servers over HTTP or another protocol" / "This is helpful for simplifying the processing on backend servers, and backend servers don't need to maintain the SSL certificate for request verification" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TLS_termination_proxy | non-official | SSL/TLS 卸载 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/elasticloadbalancing/latest/application/introduction.html | AWS official |
| 533 | Amazon Route 53 | Route 53 | "Some examples of DNS load balancers are Amazon Route 53 and Azure Traffic Manager" / "DNS-based GSLB involves analyzing the client's IP location" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | Amazon Route 53 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/Route53/latest/DeveloperGuide/routing-policy.html | AWS official |
| 534 | Nginx | nginx, nginx open source | "Nginx is a high-performance open source web server and reverse proxy server renowned for its efficiency, scalability, and versatility" / "Nginx shines as a versatile and powerful open source web server, serving as a potent LB" | https://nginx.org/en/docs/ | non-official | Nginx | https://nginx.org/en/docs/ | non-official |
| 535 | HAProxy | HAProxy load balancer | "Some examples of software LBs are HAProxy and nginx" / "Software LBs are implemented as software applications running on standard server hardware or virtual machines" | https://www.haproxy.org | non-official | HAProxy | https://www.haproxy.org | non-official |
| 536 | service-level agreement | SLA | "Users pay according to usage or the service-level agreement (SLA)" / "The key advantages of LBaaSs are ease of use, flexibility, metered costs, and robust auditing and monitoring services" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service-level_agreement | non-official | 服务级别协议（SLA） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/legal/service-level-agreements/ | AWS official |
| 537 | fault tolerance | fault tolerant | "Distributing traffic among multiple servers minimizes the risk of a single point of failure. If one server encounters issues or fails, the LB redirects traffic to healthy servers, maintaining service continuity" / "ECMP routers operate by hashing packet headers to determine the appropriate path for each packet, ensuring that traffic is evenly distributed among available links" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fault_tolerance | non-official | 容错性 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/elasticloadbalancing/latest/userguide/what-is-load-balancing.html | AWS official |

# Glossary: ch07 — Containerization, Orchestration, and Deployments

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch07.html`.

> **Note on ch07 terms:** This chapter covers containerization (Docker), container orchestration (Kubernetes), and deployment strategies. AWS service names (Amazon ECR, Amazon ECS, Amazon EKS) use their official Chinese names from AWS documentation. Kubernetes-specific terms (Pod, ReplicaSet, ConfigMap, etcd, kubelet, kube-proxy) are kept in English as per AWS EKS Chinese documentation conventions. Deployment strategy terms (blue-green, canary, rolling) use AWS CodeDeploy Chinese documentation translations. DevOps and CI/CD terms use AWS DevOps documentation translations.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 538 | containerization | containerized | "containerization has become a game changer in the world of software development and deployment" / "Containerization empowers organizations to embrace cloud native architectures, streamline development workflows, and accelerate digital transformation initiatives" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 容器化 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/containers/ | AWS official |
| 539 | container | containers | "a way to package an application along with everything it needs to run—like libraries and dependencies—into a lightweight, portable unit called a container" / "containers ensure that the application runs the same way no matter where it's deployed, whether that's on a developer's laptop, on a testing server, or in a production environment" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 容器 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/containers/ | AWS official |

# Glossary: ch09 — AWS Network Services

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch09.html`.

> **Note on ch09 terms:** This chapter covers AWS networking services including VPC, Route 53, ELB, API Gateway, and CloudFront. Many terms are already documented in earlier chapters:
> - Amazon VPC (#279), Availability Zone/AZ (#278), Internet Gateway/IGW (#399), NAT gateway (#398), NACL (#400), Transit Gateway (#396), PrivateLink (#397), Amazon Route 53 (#395, #533), Elastic Load Balancer/ELB (#387, #497), Network Load Balancer/NLB (#388), Application Load Balancer/ALB (#527), Infrastructure as Code/IaC (#384, #587), Amazon CloudFront (#125), Amazon Cognito (#277), AWS WAF (#353), AWS Shield (#155), TTL (#98), Edge locations/PoPs (#485, #595), Amazon API Gateway (#501), AWS Direct Connect (#490), DNS load balancers (#524)
> New terms unique to ch09 are added below.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 598 | AWS Region | regions, AWS regions | "AWS defines the geographically distributed locations around the world where it operates data centers as AWS regions" / "For example, the US North Virginia region is called us-east-1. Each region is a separate and independent geographic area, isolated from other regions" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | AWS 区域 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/general/latest/gr/rande-manage.html | AWS official |
| 599 | AWS Local Zones | local zones | "Local zones enable you to set up infrastructure near your customers, and the infrastructure is connected to an AWS region via a fast-paced network" / "AWS local zones are an extension of an AWS region and are designed to bring AWS services with low-latency requirements closer to specific geographic areas" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | AWS Local Zones（本地区域） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/using-regions-availability-zones.html | AWS official |
| 600 | CIDR | CIDR block, Classless Inter-Domain Routing, CIDR notation | "CIDR is a way of representing an IP address and its subnet mask. The classless concept was introduced in 1993 to slow the exhaustion of IPv4 addresses" / "The IPv4 CIDR block is a required input that must be added to proceed further. The allowed CIDR block for IPv4 is between /16 and /28" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 无类别域间路由（CIDR） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/vpc/latest/userguide/vpc-ip-addressing.html | AWS official |
| 601 | IPv4 | IPv4 address, IPv4 addresses | "IPv4 is a 32-bit or 4-byte address space where each byte is represented via decimal numbers (binary octet) and separated by a dot (.), such as 192.168.1.0" / "Both IPv4 and IPv6 addresses can be further divided into public and private IP addresses based on their visibility" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | IPv4（互联网协议第4版） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/vpc/latest/userguide/vpc-ip-addressing.html | AWS official |
| 602 | IPv6 | IPv6 address | "IPv6 is 128 bits, which provides a much larger network space as compared to 32-bit IPv4 addresses. IPv6 is represented by eight groups of four hexadecimal digits and separated by colons" / "the site prefix represents public topology allocated by an ISP or regional internet registry" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | IPv6（互联网协议第6版） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/vpc/latest/userguide/vpc-ip-addressing.html | AWS official |
| 603 | subnet | subnets, public subnet, private subnet, public subnets, private subnets | "subnet means a subnetwork: a logical subdivision of the IP address range inside the VPC CIDR block" / "a web application's servers can be placed in a public subnet so that the application is accessible to the general public via the internet, and the database servers can be placed in a private subnet to limit accessibility from the general public" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 子网（公有子网 / 私有子网） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/vpc/latest/userguide/configure-subnets.html | AWS official |
| 604 | subnet mask | subnet masks | "The subnet mask is the division of the IP address into the network and host address. It is a 32-bit number where host bits are set to 0 and network bits are set to 1" / "CIDR helps in varying subnet mask length, skipping standard division via classes" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subnet#Subnet_mask | non-official | 网络掩码（子网掩码） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/vpc/latest/userguide/vpc-ip-addressing.html | AWS official |
| 605 | elastic IP address | elastic IP, EIP, elastic IP addresses | "An elastic IP address is a static, public IPv4 address associated with an AWS account that can be assigned to an Amazon EC2 instance or network interface" / "an elastic IP address maintains the same IP address if an unhealthy EC2 instance is replaced by a healthy instance" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 弹性 IP 地址（EIP） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/vpc/latest/userguide/vpc-ip-addressing.html | AWS official |
| 606 | route table | route tables, routes, main route table, custom route table | "A route table is a collection of rules, called routes, that determine where network traffic from your subnet or gateway is directed" / "Every subnet should be associated with a route table, and the main route table is created implicitly to provide private access among the subnets" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 路由表 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/vpc/latest/userguide/configure-subnets.html | AWS official |
| 607 | security group | SG, SGs, security groups | "SGs define the rules for the traffic allowed to (and from) the resources. SGs are created at the VPC level and assigned at an instance level, controlling inbound and outbound traffic at the instance level" / "SGs are stateful. For example, you fire one request from your personal laptop to an EC2 instance to get some data" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 安全组 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/vpc/latest/userguide/vpc-security-groups.html | AWS official |
| 608 | VPC peering | VPC peering connection | "VPC peering is based on a many-to-many approach where one VPC peers with another VPC to enable full, bidirectional, private network connectivity" / "It enables resources in different VPCs to communicate with one another using private IP addresses as if they were in the same network" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | VPC 对等连接 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/vpc/latest/peering/what-is-vpc-peering.html | AWS official |
| 609 | VPC Lattice | Amazon VPC Lattice | "AWS offers VPC Lattice as a managed service to remove all this overhead and simplify application connectivity across VPCs and AWS accounts" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/vpc-lattice/latest/ug/what-is-vpc-lattice.html | official | Amazon VPC Lattice | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/vpc-lattice/latest/ug/what-is-vpc-lattice.html | AWS official |
| 610 | AWS VPN | VPN, virtual private network, VPN connection, VPN gateway, customer gateway | "The service framework that ensures this data security is VPN" / "A secure, encrypted connection channel between the customer gateway and VPN gateway. Two tunnels are set up to avoid any availability issues due to failure or scheduled maintenance" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | AWS VPN（虚拟专用网络） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/vpn/latest/s2svpn/VPC_VPN.html | AWS official |
| 611 | virtual interface | VIF, public VIF, private VIF, transit VIF | "The VIF is used to set up private, secure connections to required resources such as S3 without going over the internet. You can select public VIF, private VIF, or transit VIF depending on the use case" / "Private VIF connects to AWS resources using their private IP addresses hosted in the Amazon VPC" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 虚拟接口（VIF） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/directconnect/latest/UserGuide/private-transit-vif-example.html | AWS official |
| 612 | Classic Load Balancer | CLB | "Legacy version of an LB that supports both L4 and L7 traffic" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 经典负载均衡器（CLB） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/elasticloadbalancing/latest/classic/elb-internal-load-balancers.html | AWS official |
| 613 | Gateway Load Balancer | GWLB | "Used as an L3 gateway and L4 LB for the IP protocol" / "GWLBs are useful in systems like firewalls, intrusion detection and prevention systems, and deep packet inspection systems" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 网关负载均衡器（GWLB） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/elasticloadbalancing/latest/gateway/introduction.html | AWS official |
| 614 | shared responsibility model | shared responsibility | "AWS operates on a shared responsibility model, meaning the customer and AWS work together to make best use of services in a secure and cost-effective way, where AWS is responsible for 'security of the cloud' and customers are responsible for 'security in the cloud'" / "Cloud providers work on a shared responsibility model, which means that to ensure a good run on the cloud, customers and cloud providers must work together" | https://aws.amazon.com/compliance/shared-responsibility-model/ | official | 责任共担模型 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/elasticbeanstalk/latest/dg/platforms-shared-responsibility.html | AWS official |
| 615 | AWS Landing Zone | Landing Zone | "AWS offers the Landing Zone service, which provides a baseline to get started with multiaccount architecture, IAM, governance, data security, network design, and logging" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/controltower/latest/userguide/planning-your-deployment.html | official | AWS Landing Zone（着陆区） | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/prescriptive-guidance/latest/large-migration-foundation-playbook/landing-zone.html | AWS official |
| 616 | AWS Control Tower | Control Tower | "AWS Control Tower, can be used to set up initial prescriptive configurations, and further customizations can be made as organizations scale" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | AWS Control Tower | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/zh_cn/controltower/latest/userguide/what-is-control-tower.html | AWS official |
| 617 | dedicated tenancy | dedicated hardware, shared tenancy | "The instances can run either on shared hardware (other people's instances can run on the same hardware via virtualization) or on dedicated hardware where your instances are separated from other customer's resources" / "This option can be selected at the time of creating the VPC, and all the instances launched in the VPC are created with the same option by default" | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/dedicated-instance.html | official | 专用租赁（共享租赁） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/dedicated-instance.html | AWS official |
| 618 | Anycast | Anycast routing | "Route 53 uses Anycast as its networking and routing technology, which reduces latency by routing requests through the nearest data center and increases availability with multiple servers to respond to traffic" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anycast | non-official | 任播 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/blogs/china/dns-over-https-and-anycast-ip-enhance-the-security-and-privacy-of-domain-name-resolution/ | AWS official |
| 619 | hosted zones | hosted zone, DNS records | "DNS records of your domain are collectively stored in hosted zones to answer the domain queries" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 托管区域 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/Route53/latest/DeveloperGuide/hosted-zone-public-considerations.html | AWS official |

# Glossary: ch11 — AWS Compute Services

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch11.html`.

> **Note on ch11 terms:** This chapter covers Amazon EC2, AWS Lambda, and containerization services (ECS/EKS). Many core terms are already documented in earlier chapters:
> - Amazon EC2 (#2, #15), virtual machine/VM (#540), hypervisor (#542), virtualization (#541), container (#539), containerization (#538), Amazon ECS (#243, #550), Amazon EKS (#551), Amazon ECR (#547, #679), Kubernetes (#560), AWS Lambda (#389, #549), cold start (#103), provisioned concurrency (#408), serverless (#585), Docker (#543), control plane (#570), etcd (#575), worker node (#571), cluster (#562)
> New terms unique to ch11 are added below.

|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|

| 621 | bare-metal server | bare-metal | "Physical servers dedicated to a single tenant. | 623 | spot instances | spot instance | "Spot instances are less expensive than on-demand instances" / "these instances are made available as spot instances at a lower cost. | 624 | on-demand instances | on-demand instance | "on-demand instances are instances that we launch as needed from the AWS Console" / "Spot instances are less expensive than on-demand instances" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 按需实例 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/ec2-on-demand-instances.html | AWS official |
| 625 | reserved instances | reserved instance capacity | "cost can be reduced by using reserved EC2 instance capacity to get up to a 72% discount compared to on-demand instance pricing" / "we precommit to using EC2 instances for a certain period of time in the case of reserved instances" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glossary/latest/reference/glos-chap.html | official | 预留实例 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/ec2-reserved-instances.html | AWS official |

| 627 | cooldown period | cooldown | "we can define a cooldown period (the default is 300 seconds). # Glossary: ch13 — Big Data, Analytics, and Machine Learning Services

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch13.html`.

> **Note on ch13 terms:** This chapter covers AWS big data, analytics, and ML services. AWS service names (EMR, Glue, Athena, QuickSight, Redshift, SageMaker, and ML application services) are kept in English per AWS naming convention and marked "official" where confirmed in the AWS glossary or official service documentation. Many foundational terms (HDFS #220, MapReduce #224, Apache Spark #233, Apache Hive #225, Apache HBase #227, Presto #229, YARN #228, Amazon EMR #236, Amazon Redshift #237, Amazon Athena #238, AWS Glue #239, ETL #247, data warehouse #248, big data #251, OLAP #252, AWS Step Functions #240, Amazon Kinesis #241, Amazon MSK #242, Apache Airflow #258, A/B testing #434, Apache Parquet #347) are already documented in earlier chapters and are cross-referenced rather than duplicated here.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 638 | EMRFS (EMR File System) | EMRFS, EMR File System | "EMRFS uses Amazon S3 as a filesystem for data processing instead of a local HDFS, so essentially, it's a connector that links EMR clusters to S3" / "EMRFS ensures streaming of data directly to S3 and uses HDFS as intermediate storage" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/emr/latest/ManagementGuide/emr-fs.html | official | EMRFS（EMR 文件系统） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/emr/latest/ManagementGuide/emr-fs.html | AWS official |
| 639 | EMR bootstrap actions | bootstrap actions | "you can use EMR bootstrap actions, which execute the action after the cluster instances are up" / "There may also be a requirement to install custom software or modify a configuration for nodes on EMR nodes" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/emr/latest/ManagementGuide/emr-plan-bootstrap.html | official | 引导操作 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/emr/latest/ManagementGuide/emr-plan-bootstrap.html | AWS official |
| 640 | Apache ORC | ORC, Optimized Row Columnar | "Apache ORC are columnar file formats that give increased read performance" / "The data-access patterns can vary based on the use case. You might be interested in only a subset of columns" | https://orc.apache.org | non-official | Apache ORC（优化行列式文件格式） | https://orc.apache.org | non-official |
| 641 | Apache Avro | Avro, row-optimized file format | "Apache Avro, a row-optimized file format, is helpful for the latter use case" / "Apache Parquet and Apache ORC are columnar file formats that give increased read performance" | https://avro.apache.org | non-official | Apache Avro | https://avro.apache.org | non-official |
| 642 | S3DistCP | S3 distributed copy | "leveraging tools such as S3DistCP to copy data from S3 to HDFS or from HDFS to S3" / "You can use a combination of S3 and HDFS to address the temporary data storage limitation of HDFS" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/emr/latest/ReleaseGuide/UsingEMR_s3distcp.html | official | S3DistCP（S3 分布式复制工具） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/emr/latest/ReleaseGuide/UsingEMR_s3distcp.html | AWS official |

These tables can be useful as source and target" / "leverage the Glue data crawler and Apache Hive metastore" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glue/latest/dg/components-overview.html | official | 数据目录 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/glue/latest/dg/components-overview.html | AWS official |
| 644 | data crawler (Glue) | crawler, Glue crawler | "The data crawler crawls the data from data sources like Amazon S3, figures out the schema via the classifier, and then creates the required tables or partitions in the Glue data catalog" / "you can run the crawler on a schedule, and it automatically figures out modifications since the last run" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glue/latest/dg/add-crawler.html | official | 数据爬网程序 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/glue/latest/dg/add-crawler.html | AWS official |

| 646 | federated query | federated queries | "Athena offers a feature called federated query. | 649 | SPICE (Super-fast, Parallel, In-memory Calculation Engine) | SPICE | "QuickSight supports fast advanced calculations to serve data via SPICE (Super-fast, Parallel, In-memory Calculation Engine)" / "you should enable it as part of the database creation or editing process, and you need to configure the required SPICE capacity" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/quicksight/latest/user/spice.html | official | SPICE（超快速并行内存计算引擎） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/quicksight/latest/user/spice.html | AWS official |

| 651 | zone maps | zone map | "Redshift maintains in-memory metadata information called zone maps. It stores the minimum and maximum values for a block and effectively prunes the data blocks that don't contain data for a specific query" / "Zone maps can be optimized using a sort key" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/redshift/latest/dg/c_best-practices-sort-key.html | official | 区间映射（Zone Map） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/redshift/latest/dg/c_best-practices-sort-key.html | AWS official |
| 652 | sort key (Redshift) | sort keys | "Zone maps can be optimized using a sort key, which defines how data is sorted on the physical disk" / "The sort key works well except in a very few scenarios" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/redshift/latest/dg/t_Sorting_data.html | official | 排序键 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/redshift/latest/dg/t_Sorting_data.html | AWS official |
| 653 | distribution styles (Redshift) | distribution style, data distribution | "To optimize query performance, you can optionally choose to provide a distribution style for data. | 654 | materialized views (Redshift) | materialized view | "Redshift allows you to create materialized views from one or more tables to make the queries faster. | 655 | workload management (WLM) tools | WLM, workload management | "Redshift offers workload management (WLM) tools to separate different query workloads and assign priority so that important queries are executed and less important queries can be throttled or aborted" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/redshift/latest/dg/c_workload_mngmt_classification.html | official | 工作负载管理（WLM） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/zh_cn/redshift/latest/dg/c_workload_mngmt_classification.html | AWS official |
| 656 | Redshift Spectrum | Amazon Redshift Spectrum | "Redshift offers Redshift Spectrum, which can use S3 as storage; the compute nodes are only responsible for gathering the results. # Glossary: ch15 — Designing a Web Crawler and Search Engine

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch15.html`.

> **Note on ch15 terms:** This chapter covers web crawler and search engine system design, including URL management, indexing strategies, and AWS deployment. Many AWS services referenced in this chapter are already documented in earlier chapters: Amazon DynamoDB (#260), Amazon S3 (#4), Amazon SQS (#326), Amazon SNS (#327), Amazon MSK (#242), AWS Step Functions (#240), Amazon MWAA (#337), AWS Lambda (#389, #549), Amazon ElastiCache (#263), Amazon OpenSearch (#264), Amazon EKS (#551), Amazon Kendra (#686), Amazon SageMaker (#662), Elasticsearch (#420), Apache Lucene (#421), VPC (#279), NAT gateway (#398), Internet Gateway (#399), TTL (#98, #314), ETag (#97), Amazon Keyspaces (#266), Day 0 architecture (#381), Day N architecture (#382). New terms added below focus on web crawler/search engine domain concepts, specific algorithms, and AWS FIS.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 695 | web crawler | web crawlers, crawler, spider | "A web crawler is essentially software responsible for crawling through web content" / "The web crawler's job finishes as soon as the web page is downloaded" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_crawler | non-official | 网络爬虫 | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%BD%91%E7%BB%9C%E8%9C%98%E8%9B%9B | official (other source) |
| 696 | search engine | search engines | "The search engine sits on top of content accumulated by web crawlers and stores it in such a way that it can look for user-searched keywords" / "The search engine should be able to figure out the best matching results for the user search query" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Search_engine | non-official | 搜索引擎 | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E6%90%9C%E7%B4%A2%E5%BC%95%E6%93%8E | official (other source) |
| 697 | seed URLs | seed URL list | "the list of URLs that the system should start with (the seed URLs) for crawling the web" / "There is no unified source that can be used to retrieve all the URLs to be used in the seed URL list" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_crawler | non-official | 种子URL | https://interview.apachecn.org/sys-design-inter/08/ | non-official |
| 698 | URL frontier | URL frontier system | "The system responsible for determining the next URL to be crawled in the web crawler architecture is generally called the URL frontier" / "The URL frontier publishes URLs to a single SNS topic, and it can route subscribed SQS FIFO queues based on priority attributes in the message" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_crawler | non-official | URL调度器（URL Frontier） | https://interview.apachecn.org/sys-design-inter/08/ | non-official |
| 699 | forward index | forward indexes, forward index creator | "The forward index stores the data, and then it is passed to the inverted index for reverse mapping" / "The forward index is a mapping of the doc ID to a set of words or keywords" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Search_engine_indexing | non-official | 正向索引 | https://www.itshujia.com/read/elasticsearch/353 | non-official |
| 700 | inverted index | inverted indexes, inverted index reader, inverted index creator | "The inverted index is an inverse mapping of the forward index containing the mapping from keywords to doc ID" / "The inverted index reader application downloads the inverted indexes created by the inverted index creator system" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inverted_index | non-official | 倒排索引 | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%80%92%E6%8E%92%E7%B4%A2%E5%BC%95 | official (other source) |
| 701 | Bloom filter | Bloom filters | "Using Bloom filters is another technique for determining whether a URL was visited or not" / "It is possible that the Bloom filter returns yes for an already-crawled URL, causing a recrawl of the URL" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloom_filter | non-official | 布隆过滤器 | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E5%B8%83%E9%9A%86%E8%BF%87%E6%BB%A4%E5%99%A8 | official (other source) |
| 702 | Merkle tree | Merkle trees | "One such solution is a Merkle tree (discussed in ch03), which efficiently evaluates the updated section" / "it is generally used by databases like Dynamo to resolve anti-entropy among multiple replicas" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merkle_tree | non-official | 默克尔树 | https://yeasy.gitbook.io/blockchain_guide/05_crypto/merkle_trie | non-official |
| 703 | politeness | crawl politeness, politeness rate limiting | "The TTL factor in web crawler terms is referred to as politeness, meaning how frequently a crawler fetches content from the web page server" / "Back queues enforce politeness of the URL to ensure that the web server is not bombarded too frequently" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_crawler | non-official | 爬取礼貌性（Politeness） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%BD%91%E7%BB%9C%E8%9C%98%E8%9B%9B | non-official |
| 704 | content freshness | data freshness | "The system needs to ensure content freshness so that it stores the most relevant data to offer the ideal search experience" / "The underlying storage to serve search queries should be regularly updated" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freshness_(information_retrieval) | non-official | 内容新鲜度 | https://blog.csdn.net/2501_91930600/article/details/148503625 | non-official |
| 705 | robots.txt | robots exclusion standard | "The crawler will also respect any exclusions on the web server configured in a robots.txt file" / "The robots.txt file determines whether a web page is allowed for crawling or whether any specific resource is not to be indexed by search engines" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robots.txt | non-official | robots.txt（机器人排除协议） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robots.txt | non-official |
| 706 | DRUM algorithm | disk repository with update management | "IRLBot suggests the disk repository with the update management (DRUM) algorithm as an algorithm for ensuring URL uniqueness" / "DRUM maintains k memory arrays, each of size M, and k disk buckets (Qi) and spreads key-value pairs between them based on the key" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_crawler | non-official | DRUM算法（磁盘存储库与更新管理算法） | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_crawler | non-official |
| 707 | STAR algorithm | spam tracking and avoidance through reputation | "IRLBot solves this via the spam tracking and avoidance through reputation (STAR) algorithm" / "The reputation of a page is calculated based on in-degree links from other domain web pages" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_crawler | non-official | STAR算法（基于信誉的垃圾追踪与规避算法） | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_crawler | non-official |
| 708 | BEAST algorithm | budget enforcement with anti-spam tactics | "This is solved by the budget enforcement with anti-spam tactics (BEAST) algorithm" / "BEAST maintains an additional queue to all such URLs and keeps reevaluating their relevance to figure out when the URLs should be crawled" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_crawler | non-official | BEAST算法（具备反垃圾策略的爬取预算执行算法） | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_crawler | non-official |
| 709 | Simhash algorithm | Simhash | "Google crawler uses the Simhash algorithm to help figure out content duplication of web pages" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SimHash | non-official | Simhash算法 | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SimHash | non-official |
| 710 | chaos engineering | — | "This practice is called chaos engineering: the discipline of experimenting on a system to build confidence in the system's capability to withstand turbulent conditions in production" / "The experiment can be as simple as an EC2 instance being down; is it replaced by another one?" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaos_engineering | non-official | 混沌工程 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/blogs/china/aws-chaos-engineering-start/ | AWS official |
| 711 | AWS Fault Injection Service | FIS, AWS FIS | "In the AWS cloud environment, you could use AWS Fault Injection Service (FIS)" / "A lot of open source alternatives are also available for running these experiments" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/fis/latest/userguide/what-is.html | official | AWS 故障注入服务（AWS FIS） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/fis/ | AWS official |
| 712 | PageRank | page rank, page relevance score | "The content relevance system (such as PageRank) computes the most relevant results and returns them to the user" / "Many more attributes can be used to determine a page rank" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PageRank | non-official | PageRank（网页排名算法） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/PageRank | non-official |
| 713 | refresh intervals | refresh interval | "Each shard consists of multiple segments, which are kept in memory (and a transaction log on disk to overcome failures) and are synced to disk in fixed time intervals (referred to as refresh intervals)" / "If a use case requires data to be available for search as soon as it is written, the refresh interval can be configured to a lower value" | https://www.elastic.co/guide/en/elasticsearch/reference/current/index-modules.html | non-official | 刷新间隔 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/opensearch-service/latest/developerguide/what-is.html | non-official |
| 714 | document-based partitioning | document-based | "All the terms from a document are stored on a single machine, offering the benefit of calculating page relevance easily" / "Based on these trade-offs, we'll choose document-based partitioning to maintain the inverted indexes" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_(database) | non-official | 基于文档的分区 | https://blog.csdn.net/m0_37739193/article/details/119300816 | non-official |
| 715 | term-based partitioning | term-based | "assume the system has extracted 100 terms out of the crawled web pages. These 100 terms are divided into a set of machines: 10 machines with 10 terms each" / "The downside of this approach is creating an intersection of search results because the terms corresponding to the same document can reside on different machines" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_(database) | non-official | 基于词项的分区 | https://blog.csdn.net/m0_37739193/article/details/119300816 | non-official |
| 716 | multipart upload | multipart download, multipart upload and download | "Multipart upload and download ensure uploading of the large files in chunks to provide better reliability and ease in reuploading or redownloading a smaller chunk if the operation fails" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/mpuoverview.html | official | 分段上传（分段下载） | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/mpuoverview.html | AWS official |
| 717 | content relevance | search result relevance, content relevance system | "The content relevance system (such as PageRank) computes the most relevant results" / "The content relevance system consists of multiple applications to figure out the relevance of search results and can use services such as Amazon SageMaker for running any ML algorithms" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relevance_(information_retrieval) | non-official | 内容相关性 | https://blog.csdn.net/2501_91930600/article/details/148503625 | non-official |
| 718 | anchor text | anchors | "All the important information about links, such as where the link is pointing from and to and the text of the link, is stored in data structures referred to as anchors" / "Putting the anchor text into the forward index barrel associated with the doc ID that the anchor is pointed to" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchor_text | non-official | 锚文本 | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E9%94%9A%E6%96%87%E6%9C%AC | non-official |
| 719 | doc ID | document ID | "Assigning an identification, called a doc ID, to any new URLs (web links) found by parsing the web pages" / "Converting every word into a word ID using an in-memory hash table called a lexicon" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_crawler | non-official | 文档ID（doc ID） | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_crawler | non-official |
| 720 | lexicon | in-memory hash table (word IDs) | "Converting every word into a word ID using an in-memory hash table called a lexicon" / "The hits include the actual word, the position of the word in the document, the approximate font size, and capitalization" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lexicon | non-official | 词典（lexicon） | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lexicon | non-official |

# Glossary: ch17 — Designing an Online Game Leaderboard

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch17.html`.

> **Note on ch17 terms:** Many core AWS services (Amazon DynamoDB, ElastiCache, SQS, SNS, API Gateway, CloudWatch, Lambda, ECS, Fargate, CloudFront, AppSync, S3 Glacier, VPC, IAM, Cognito, Shield) are documented in earlier chapters. This section adds new terms introduced specifically in ch17: TCO cost components, algorithmic data structures (skip list, balanced BST, external merge sort, consistent hashing), disaster recovery metrics (RPO, RTO, PITR), gaming-specific terms (MMORPG), ElastiCache Global Datastore, AWS WAF, Aurora Serverless, AWS CodePipeline, tooling (Jira, Opsgenie, AWS Pricing Calculator).

|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 721 | total cost of ownership (TCO) | TCO, TCO analysis | "The TCO is the cost associated with operating a product throughout its lifecycle. There are multiple components of TCO" / "We should take a holistic view to figure out the costs associated with a system to avoid any surprises" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_cost_of_ownership | non-official | 总拥有成本（TCO） | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/tco-calculator/ | AWS official |

# Glossary: ch19 — Designing a Chat Application

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch19.html`.

> **Note on ch19 terms:** This chapter covers chat application design (WhatsApp-style), covering messaging protocols, actor model, Erlang/Ejabberd architecture, and AWS Day 0/Day N deployment. Many core terms are already documented in earlier chapters: XMPP (#163), MQTT (#164), WebSockets (#169), GraphQL (#176), AWS AppSync (#177), Amazon SQS (#326), Amazon SNS (#327), AWS WAF (#353/#729), Amazon Cognito (#277), Amazon CloudWatch (#325), Amazon DynamoDB (#260), Amazon ElastiCache (#137/#263/#728), Amazon S3 (#4), Amazon ECS (#243), Amazon CloudFront (#125), AWS Shield (#155), chaos engineering (#710), AWS Fault Injection Service (#711), Aurora Limitless Database (#447), DynamoDB global tables (#409), Amazon API Gateway (#501), ALB (#527), NLB (#388/#526), ELB (#497), congestion avoidance (#152). New terms introduced specifically in ch19 are listed below.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 737 | actor model | actor, actors, actor pattern, supervisor actor | "The actor model is a conceptual model proposed to support large numbers of connections concurrently with minimal resources" / "An actor is a very small object and acts as a fundamental unit of computation in the system" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Actor_model | non-official | Actor 模型 | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/Actor%E6%A8%A1%E5%9E%8B | official (other source) |
| 738 | Ejabberd | ejabberd, Ejabberd open source software | "Ejabberd is open source software specifically designed to support building near real-time chat applications. The software is built in Erlang and can be deployed as an XMPP server" / "Ejabberd software can support more than two million concurrent users on a single machine" | https://www.ejabberd.im | non-official | Ejabberd | https://www.ejabberd.im | non-official |
| 739 | Erlang | Erlang programming language, Erlang processes, Erlang cluster | "Erlang is a functional programming language with built-in support of actors and is specifically designed to handle a huge number of concurrent connections" / "Erlang offers lightweight processes with very minimal resources as compared to processes from other programming languages" | https://www.erlang.org | non-official | Erlang | https://www.erlang.org | non-official |
| 740 | BEAM (Bogdan's Erlang Abstract Machine) | BEAM | "Erlang uses BEAM (originally Bogdan's Erlang Abstract Machine) as a VM to schedule and run the programs in the Erlang runtime environment" / "With Erlang, the scheduling is fully managed by BEAM to optimize the overall working of the system" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BEAM_(Erlang_virtual_machine) | non-official | BEAM（Erlang 虚拟机） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/BEAM | non-official |

The AOF file is like a mailbox that holds letters until they are picked up" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Append-only | non-official | 仅追加文件（AOF） | https://blog.csdn.net/ztchun/article/details/90636768 | non-official |

| 752 | congestion (system) | system congestion, traffic congestion, overload | "Congestion basically means the system is bombarded with more requests than it can actually handle, making the system use all of its resources without performing any actual work of serving the requests" / "This situation is similar to road traffic: the actual distance covered by a vehicle reduces as the number of vehicles on the road increases" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Network_congestion | non-official | 系统拥塞 | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E7%BD%91%E7%BB%9C%E6%8B%A5%E5%A1%9E | non-official |

# Glossary: ch21 — Designing an Online Stock-Trading Platform

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `ch21.html`.

> **Note on ch21 terms:** This chapter covers the design of an online stock-trading (stockbroker) platform, including real-time stock tick delivery, the order management system (OMS), P&L dashboards, and ultra-low-latency design for high-frequency trading (HFT). AWS services already documented in earlier chapters (Amazon Timestream #265, AWS AppSync #177, AWS Direct Connect #490, Amazon MSK #242, Amazon Aurora #6, Amazon DynamoDB #260, Amazon Redshift #237, Amazon QuickSight #430, AWS Batch #366, AWS Step Functions #240, AWS Local Zones #599, AWS Outposts #636, VPC peering #608, AWS Transit Gateway #396, Protocol Buffers #746, Akka #757, goroutines #754, FPGA #677, Day 0/N architecture #381/#382, idempotency #253) are not duplicated here. New terms introduced in this chapter cover financial domain concepts (stock exchange, stockbroker, order types, FIX protocol, market depth), latency-optimization concepts (off-heap memory, GC, placement groups, Nitro SSD, warm pools, Wavelength), and new AWS infrastructure options.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 765 | stock exchange | stock exchanges, NYSE, NSE, Nasdaq | "Stock exchanges are marketplaces where stocks are bought and sold. A few examples include the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) in the US and the National Stock Exchange (NSE) of India" / "The trading hours for the Nasdaq stock exchange are 9:30 A.M. to 4:00 P.M. EST" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stock_exchange | non-official | 证券交易所（NYSE 纽约证券交易所；Nasdaq 纳斯达克；NSE 印度国家证券交易所） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%AF%81%E5%88%B8%E4%BA%A4%E6%98%93%E6%89%80 | official (other source) |
| 766 | stockbroker | stockbrokers, stockbroker application, broker application | "These platforms, called stockbrokers, work as intermediaries between stock exchanges and users" / "This chapter focuses on the design of a stockbroker application, not a stock exchange application" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stockbroker | non-official | 股票经纪商（券商；经纪平台） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E8%82%A1%E7%A5%A8%E7%BB%8F%E7%BA%AA%E4%BA%BA | official (other source) |
| 767 | order execution | order, executing trades, order execution system | "Buying or selling a stock is called an order. The order is executed on the customer's behalf by the stockbroker" / "The buying and selling of any stock is generally referred to as order execution in the trading world" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_(exchange) | non-official | 委托执行（订单执行） | https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E5%A7%94%E6%89%98%E6%89%A7%E8%A1%8C/10784561 | non-official |
| 768 | candlestick chart | candlestick charts | "Users can view candlestick charts of any stock over the requested time period" / "The exchanges also offer snapshots of historical data to broker applications, which can be used for use cases like candlestick charts" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candlestick_chart | non-official | K线图（蜡烛图） | https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh/K%E7%BA%BF | official (other source) |
| 769 | P&L (Profit and Loss) | P&L, profit and loss, P&L dashboard | "Users can view their portfolios with profit and loss (P&L) overall and for individual stocks" / "The P&L dashboard shows the user's stock holdings at any point in time, which consists of the P&L of each individual stock as well as the overall portfolio" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_statement | non-official | 盈亏（P&L；损益） | https://baike.baidu.com/item/%E7%9B%88%E4%BA%8F | non-official |

# Glossary: Foreword

All AWS terminology and technical glossary terms identified from `foreword.html`.

> **Note on foreword terms:** The foreword is a short endorsement by Swami Sivasubramanian (VP, AI and Data at AWS). It mentions two core distributed-systems concepts (CAP theorem, blast radius) and two AWS services (S3, DynamoDB) that are already documented in earlier chapters (S3 #4, DynamoDB #260). Only new terms are added here.

| # | term (EN) | variations (EN) | examples (EN) | term citation | term type | translation | translation citation | translation type |
|---|-----------|-----------------|---------------|---------------|-----------|-------------|----------------------|-----------------|
| 803 | CAP theorem | CAP, Brewer's theorem | "covering the basics of trade-offs in distributed systems, such as the CAP theorem, units of blast radius" / "concepts like CAP theorem, blast radius, and seamless scalability were not well understood" | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CAP_theorem | non-official | CAP 定理 | https://aws.amazon.com/cn/builders-library/avoiding-fallback-in-distributed-systems/ | non-official |
| 804 | blast radius | units of blast radius | "covering the basics of trade-offs in distributed systems, such as the CAP theorem, units of blast radius" / "concepts like CAP theorem, blast radius, and seamless scalability were not well understood" | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/reliability-pillar/rel_fault_isolation_limit_blast_radius.html | official | 爆炸半径 | https://docs.amazonaws.cn/wellarchitected/latest/reliability-pillar/rel_fault_isolation_limit_blast_radius.html | AWS official |

## Chapter 04 translation-review clarifications (2026-10-31)

These notes supplement the shared glossary; existing canonical entries above remain unchanged pending verification.

| Term (EN) | Approved Chinese | Note |
|---|---|---|
| most recently used (MRU) | 最近使用（MRU） | Chapter 04 editorial decision; reconcile with earlier glossary entries if needed. |
| least frequently recently used (LFRU) | 最近最不经常使用（LFRU） | Chapter 04 editorial decision; author/terminology check may still be useful. |
